## Jarbarkas

Jarbarkas was a skilled fighter who aided the dwarf resistance in Llorkh in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
Jarbarkas had caramel-colored shoulder-length hair and wore an orange headband. The muscular man wore a gold pendant and a green outfit.

The strong warrior wore chainmail and fought with a greatsword, as well as a composite longbow. He was versed in both fighting at close range with his two-handed sword, and at far range with his bow.

History
Jarbarkas grew up in the village known as Windycliffs along the Sword Coast. In 1358 DR, both his village and Leilon were pillaged by Luskar pirates. The buccaneers went even further, and burned many houses, including his own. The warrior swore revenge on the pirates, and set out to aid the Lords' Alliance. Jarbarkas signed on as part of a Waterdhavian volunteer force to aid the dwarves of Llorkh, who were heavily oppressed by the Zhentarim.

When the volunteer force arrived in Llorkh, they were ambushed by the Zhentarim, and taken prisoner. Jarbarkas was questioned by Lord Geildarr Ithym himself, but did not give any information over. A giant skeleton was animated to kill the fighter, but he was saved by the Heroes of Ascore and the dwarven resistance force. He informed the group about the magical crystals used by the Zhentarim and their allies, and advised the heroes to search their bodies carefully. After this, he asked to join the heroes on their mission.Jarbarkas and his female counterpart, Siulajia, were the first ever NPCs in a video game to be able to fall in love with a player character. Thus, they only joined the group if the party leader was of the opposite sex.

## Jaluth Alaerth

Jaluth Alaerth, known as "Snakeface", was one of the Overwizards of the Arcane Brotherhood in the 1360s DR.

Description
In her middle age, Jaluth was woman of cruel beauty who possessed hot temper.

Powers
Jaluth had a strange power or curse that caused her face to spill snakes every time that she was angry or upset.

Activities
Circa the, Jaluth was developing new necromantic spells in order to gain control of undead, golems, and gargoyles. She plotted to gain control over all dragons, conquer Icewind Dale, take possession of as many mines as possible, and also sack dwarven holdings and Netheril's ruins. By 1373 DR, Jaluth found and claimed a small Netherese ruin within The Giants' Walls of the Reghed Glacier.

History
In the, after Eldeluc and Valkebar's coup against the Archmage Arcane, Arklem Greeth, Jaluth pledged her loyalty to the conspirators. When Greeth returned, now transformed into a lich with some new devilish allies, Jaluth relocated to near Icewind Dale.

In the, Jaluth ventured into The Giants' Walls of the Reghed Glacier in order to unlock true power of a mysterious crystal the came across. In reality, the crystal was a shard from the body of Arctus, a godling son of Auril, imprisoned in the Reghed Glacier. Jaluth was protected by animated entombed under her magical control. The wizard found Assyntia, an ice weird, who resided in the middle of a frozen lake who possessed the knowledge of the shard's true nature. The weird, however, refused to help the evil mage. The Hero of Neverwinter and their companions stumbled across Jaluth Alaerth on their quest to stop the doom of Icewind Dale, caused by the looming threat of Arctus. She offered the Hero a deal to help get the information from the stubborn ice weird. The Hero agreed and the weird offered them the knowledge of the crystal and warned not to pass that information along to the evil mage. It was unknown if the Hero of Neverwinter confronted Jaluth and was attacked by her entombed or if they shared the weird's knowledge with her in exchange for a set of powerful enchanted armor – Chain of Drakkas. Jaluth sought to try and bend the imprisoned godling's power to her will.

## Jaws of the wolf

Jaws of the wolf was a divine evocation spell that conjured forth the spectral maw of a wolf that assaulted its target.

Effects
When cast, this spell manifested a set of fanged jaws, about the width of a human head, that streaked unerringly towards a targeted individual. When it struck, the jaws of the wolf bit the target and drained them of their life force before fading away.

Druids used a slightly different version of this spell. Instead of jaws, their version of the spell manifested a whole wolf. The amount of wolves manifested was determined by their skill level.

History
The specifications of this spell were inscribed within the relic known as the Leaves of Green.

Components
In order to be cast, this spell required verbal and somatic components along with four teeth taken from a wolf. When cast by a druid, they required a focus, a carving of a wolf, one for each manifested.

The bay was much deeper than it was wide, spanning 20 miles (32 kilometers) across and twice that distance inland. It was concealed by mist that poured down from the mouth of the River Tath.

## Jahaka Anchorage

Jahaka Anchorage was a 15 century port settlement located in a secluded sea cave in the Jahaka Bay along the western shore of Chult. It served as the secret anchorage for a group of pirates that raided ships in the waters surrounding Chult.

Description
Beyond the pier, the anchorage house some living quarters, a makeshift prison, storage areas with a well-protected trove and a tavern called Bosco's Bilge.

Activities
The pirates used Jahaka Anchorage to store their plunder and hold their captive slaves. Their respective captains formed a tenuous alliance with Commander Liara Portyr of Fort Beluarian, granting a portion of their wealth along with safe passage for Baldurian ships in exchange for a blind eye to their operations.

Government
The settlement was led by the captain of whichever ship was docked in the port. Fittingly, only one ship dropped anchor at any given time.

Defenses
Any ship nearing the anchorage had to navigate through heavy mist and a perilous circle of underwater rocks. Beyond that, the anchorage was protected by a ballista mounted upon the lookout tower constructed atop Bosco's Bilge.

The anchorage's treasure trove was protected by ten flying swords.

Inhabitants
The settlement was the safehouse for and temporary residence of a numbers of pirates, including those that served as crewmen aboard the Dragonfang, the Stirge and the Emerald Eye.

A number of reef sharks inhabited the waters surrounding the sea cave.

## Jathiman Dagger

The Jathiman Dagger was a minor artifact created by the Cult of Jathiman to slay the gods themselves.

Description
It took the form of an ordinary iron blade, weighing . It was said to ripple with arcane energy.

Powers
It was a +5 unholy dagger. Moreover, it bestowed on the wielder a particular power over the fates of divine beings, and penetrated all protections a target being had by virtue of the divine.

History
Of Netherese origin, the ancient Jathimites despised deities and all aspects of the divine. They believed that, through sheer brute force, mortals could even bring down and kill the gods. Thus, by weaving arcane magic through violent self-destruction—in the actual self-sacrifice of thirty-nine Jathimites—they manifested these forces in physical form, crafting the Jathiman Dagger.

They would not get to savor their creation for long. The god Jergal, Lord of the End of Everything, caused the slaughter of the whole surviving sect in grisly fashion: trapping them in a great coliseum where he slowly imploded and then reanimated them as ghouls who would devour the others, and forcing them to watch each other's fates in horror and hunger. Afterward, he took the Jathiman Dagger for himself.

But, rather than use it, Jergal ultimately let the Jathiman Dagger come into the possession of Bane whilst he was still a mortal. The three adventurers Bane, Bhaal, and Myrkul would go on to use the Jathiman Dagger in their unholy quest.

In the, they used it to slay the god-like being Borem, at the Lake of Boiling Mud in Rezamark, and with that act attained a measure of divinity themselves.

Later, in the, they surprised Haask, priest-king of Grong-Haap, in his throne-room in Ironfang Keep. Bhaal thrust the Jathiman Dagger into the back and heart of Haask, draining his life energy and revealing the greathorn minotaur to be truly a batrachi-doppelganger.The history here seems contradictory, as the dates imply the three slew Borem before Haask, yet left the dagger in Borem's heart, suggesting he was slain last. It may be that they stuck the dagger back in Borem's Quagheart after slaying Haask, perhaps to prevent the Lake of Boiling Mud reforming.

They left the Jathiman Dagger piercing Borem's Quagheart, which was still slowly beating, in order to prevent the reforming of the Lake of Boiling Mud and the potential return of Borem. They buried the two artifacts in a 3-foot-wide (1 meter) spherical chamber, buried some  deep on Mezeketh Isle off the coast of Sembia, at a site marked by a certain menhir inscribed in an ancient Jhaamdathan script. There it still lay, unknown to all mortals, by 1372 DR.

Notable Owners
 Jergal
 Bane

## Jalartan's miraculum

Jalartan's miraculum was an alteration wizard spell.

Effects
The caster would use this spell to alter another spell they had memorized. Jalartan's miraculum allowed the caster to cast another spell without needing one of that spell's primary components, allowing the caster to forgo either physical gestures, spoken incantations, or the need to use a physical spell component. If the spell only needed one of these components to cast then the caster would be able to cast the spell as a silent act of will.

There was no time limit that needed to be met after casting this spell for it to affect the caster's next spell.

Components
This spell only required somatic components to cast.

## Jacob

Jacob was a warrior devotee of Tyr who was a part of the rescue team sent to the Utter East to rescue Piergeiron the Paladinson's bride circa 1371 DR

Description
Jacob had blond curly hair.

Personality
Jacob was charming and charismatic, often flirty. He loved the thrill of adventure, especially when questing for the glory of Tyr.

Possessions
Unlike most other followers of Tyr, Jacob was proficient with and carried a two-handed sword.

Relationships
Prior to the rescue mission, Jacob was acquainted with Aleena Paladinstar and her father Piergeiron the Paladinson, often monster hunting in the Western Heartlands with the Open Lord of Waterdeep.

History
Jacob was one of the paladins from Phlan attending the wedding ceremony for the Open Lord of Waterdeep.

The wedding was a disaster that resulted in the bride's abduction. Jacob was a part of the rescue team assembled by Khelben Arunsun to travel through Undermountain to reach the only portal to the Utter East that was in the vicinity of the city. Blackstaff was unable to teleport the paladins to the portal directly because of Kern's anti-magical abilities, leaving the party with only one choice—traveling through Skullport.

When the paladins reached Skullport, the group was tasked with aiding zombies by the mysterious Skulls of Skullport in penance for destroying one of the watchers. The group split apart in search of undead to aid. Jacob was looking for a zombie to aid along the Skullport's pier, hoping to quickly help with moving crates. Once he came across a group of undead unloading crates, Jacob quickly volunteered to help with the job. The young warrior felt pity towards the zombies and regretted he did not possess the abilities of a paladin to free the cursed creatures. Caught in daydreaming of his glorious future among the Hammers of Tyr or Knights of the Holy Sword, Jacob was ambushed by one of the zombies who turned out to be a doppelganger. The shapeshifter overpowered the surprised warrior shoving its liquified hand in Jacob's mouth making it ooze down his throat...

Unknown to the paladins, Jacob was replaced by a doppelganger, who continued onto the rescue mission in his place.

## Jaguar

Jaguars were large, jungle-dwelling felines.

Description
Jaguars were similar in size and appearance to leopards, but were somewhat bulkier and had spotted rosettes. They had golden fur and black spots, but a rare few had black fur and black spots, and were called black jaguars.

Combat
Jaguars were ferocious predators, and greatly feared by any humanoid who lived in their territory. They were aggressive and attacked any creature they saw as a potential threat. They stealthily stalked prey, usually from above in the tree cover. Their sharp claws delivered devastating wounds. A jaguar was capable of jumping a distance of up to .

Ecology
Jaguars lived solitary lives and were primarily tree-dwelling, spending most of their time above ground.
Diets
Jaguars were obligate carnivores. Despite spending a significant part of their day in the tree tops, jaguars were excellent swimmers. Being solitary creatures, jaguars only mingled with others of their species for mating. A female jaguar had a litter of up to three cubs.
Habitats
Jaguars were typically found in jungles and were very territorial. In the Shining South, they were known to inhabit the Ajmer Forest and Forest of Amtar.

Beyond Faerûn, jaguars were found throughout Maztica and in the jungles of Zakhara. In east Zakhara, they could be found in the Grey Jungle.

Relationships
Jaguars had a significant place in Maztica, even having a month named after them in the Maztican calendar. They were the namesake of the Jaguar Knights of Maztica, and a holy symbol of the god of war, Zaltec.

Jaguar claws were a token of hishnashapers and used as a material component in certain hishna spells. Mazticans used tough strands extracted from jaguar guts to craft snares to capture parrots and other birds used for pluma magic.

Wild halfling villages in the jungles of Far Payit kept jaguars, using them to kill any prisoners.

Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold jaguar hides for 25 gp a piece.

Trivia
 The spell conjure animals could summon a jaguar to the aid of its caster.

## Jassa's Dagger

Jassa's Dagger was a dagger-shaped constellation.

Description
This constellation consisted of a line of six stars, going from the large pink star known as the Pommel down to the handle and southwest-pointing blade of the dagger, intersected through the third star from the top by a line of four stars known as the Guards.

Observation
The constellation could be observed from Twilight Tor around the same time as the Simril festivities.

Significance
It was thought that people born under Jassa's Dagger tended to have great personal pride, as well as a tendency to hold grudges. Especially ambitious people and vendettists were often linked to this constellation.

History
Legend had it that this constellation was once the dagger of Queen Jassa, ruler of the Jhaamdathan city Lirremar. When her reign came to an end due to her impalement on the end of a spear at the Battle of the Biting Serpent, the queen threw her dagger into the night sky, where it remained as a constellation.

## Jansen

The Jansens were a family of gnomes that lived across the west coast of Faerûn during the 14 century DR.

Activities
While members of the family held a number of professions, including inventors, adventurers, criminals, performers, and herbalists, their primary interests were in the cultivation and use of turnips.

Members
The Amnian Jansens were a diverse and eccentric group of gnomes.

Among them were the siblings and siblings-in-law;
 Jan Jansen, an inventor and adventurer who lived in Athkatla
 Elgar Buttercup, Jan's brother, whose skin was supposedly made of charcoal
 Burt Wonderkind, the griffon-baiter

Their cousins;
 Glandig, who had twins named Tot and Tat
 Josephine, who became involved with a "gully dwarf"
 Beeloo, another cousin who had a history of run–ins with the law
 Richard, an uncle who tried to make nude theater popular in Waterdeep
 Kylie Jansen, a turnip peddler from Athkatla
 Tat and Tot Jansen, twins from Athkatla

The aunts and uncles:
 Sara, who attempted to take over the world with turnip pies laced with psychogenic herbs
 Gerhardt, who despite his incoherent ramblings was somehow privy to the activities of the Hidden
 Hans Jansen, a great-uncle who was an accomplished alchemist
 Furnbottom Jansen, an aging adventurer whose career was ended by a hungry griffon

As well as:
 Yanna Jojannsen, Jan Jansen's niece, who was charmed by the vampire Hexxat and died while searching for the tomb of Dragomir
 Eduardo, an inventor whose creations were displayed in Waukeen's Promenade

The "Culver Jansens" were those from the family who were exiled from Thunder Mountain and settled in the city of Culver.

 Uncle Pinto, who sold the rights of Culver for a bag of turnips
 Erb, a courtesan and former chest-maker who settled in the city of Neverwinter

History
According to their family the city of Culver has not existed since the "great Turnip Famine of '45".

## Jasha Istor

Jasha Istor was a priestess of Auril in the late-14 century DR.

Abilities
Jasha Istor was known to possess a variety of spells whose effects related to providing protection against severe cold and winds.

Activities
Jasha Istor lived in and operated the Icedawn's House, a cabin shrine to the goddess of winter Auril situated on the Cloud Peaks mountains close to the town of Nashkel. She would greet every traveler that came through the area, seeking donations that she would scatter in the snow to ward off the wrath of Auril. People who stole more than one of these scattered coins in the summer were known to often die from frostbite the following winter.

She was also an expert of navigating the Cloud Peaks, considered by some to be one of the best navigating them, and offered to guide travelers through them for a steep fee.

## Jasmine (plant)

Jasmine was a fragrant flowering plant that grew in shrubs and vines and often used in perfumes and aromatics.

Description
A jasmine shrub flowered abundantly. Each small white flower had four or five long petals. Jasmine plan flowers had gentle blue hue in the spring.

Uses
Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold jasmine oil (3 gp per ), Jasmine Night perfume (1 gp per vial), and jasmine-scented water (35 sp per ))

Inhabitants of Myth Drannor used jasmine plants to create delicate and alluring perfumes. One such inhabitant, Elena radiated the aroma of jasmine long after death while haunting the ruins of the city.

Jasmine dust was an aromatic perfume-like powder imported from Kara-Tur.

Circa 1367 DR, the Palace of the Enlightened Throne in Zakhara was purchasing copious amounts of jasmine of the Grand Caliph's Private Baths after he started fancying the flower's aroma.

Circa 1367 DR, the Jasmine Dream inn was located in the city of Dihliz, owned by a gnome named Ahmad. The inn was poshly decorated and abundantly perfumed with fresh jasmine flowers.

## Jarial

Jarial was a young sorcerer who was cursed by his fiancé to remain entombed in a rock while awake and alive for many years. He remained trapped in Myth Drannor's Dwarven Dungeons until a group of adventurers from Phlan rescued him in 1369 DR.

Description
The young sorcerer was of medium built, had long brown hair, and intensive dark eyes.

Jarial was a young man of only twenty years old when he was trapped in a stone. The curse kept him alive and unaging for countless years until he was rescued.

Jarial wore a royal purple robe tied with a yellow sash.

Personality
Jarial was a young, intelligent, but inexperienced man. He, like his lover Ozama, had a sharp tongue and a short fuse. He did secretly feel jealous of Ozama's potent magical skills. That explosive part of his personality smoothed significantly after he spent years trapped in stone, contemplating his actions that led to the situation.

Abilities
Jarial possessed a gift of sorcery; even though he was inexperienced, he had access to spells like protection from evil, mage armor, magic missile, resistance, light, disrupt undead, detect magic, and daze.

Possessions
Being young and relatively inexperienced, he was cursed while only carrying a simple dagger and a quarterstaff on his person.

History
In 1353 DR, a young sorcerer Jarial met and fell madly in love with a hot-tempered wizard, Ozama. She had an insatiable lust for magic that led the young couple to Myth Drannor's undercity in search of the collector of magic, Preybelish. There, they had a terrible fight and after Jarial said some terrible things to her, Ozama tossed an enchanted pebble at Jarial's feet. Momentarily, the pebble grew, swallowing the young sorcerer's legs, leaving his torso and arms free. The wizard left him with a riddle that was the solution to his problem and despite Jarial's begging, she stormed off, leaving him alone withing a dark and empty dungeon room.

Jarial kept his sanity while staying trapped for many years inside a rock with no way of breaking the curse that also kept him alive, unaging, and invulnerable. For years he was hoping to see Ozama's return to rescue him, but that never happened. Through the years, Jarial wished many times that Ozama had dropped a boulder on his head and killed him instead of the state he was trapped in.

Once, during his years of torture, a beholder came across the curious little human. The floating aberration spent a week flinging its spells at the trapped man, before it was reduced to tears in frustration. It gave up and left Jarial to his misery.

The solution of the riddle was a ring. In 1369 DR, a group of heroes from Phlan seeking to stop the Cult of the Dragon's nefarious plan of domination came across the trapped man. He begged the Veiled Ones to free him while reciting the riddle. Thankfully, the group was equipped with rings, and they freed Jarial, who promised to aid them in the quest against the cult. His heart was still beating for the cruel wizard, and he was searching for her or her remains while traveling through the ruins with the adventurers.

Eventually, Jarial reunited with Ozama, long after she was killed by the monstrosities of Myth Drannor. Jarial grieved her death, while cradling her skeleton that still had her belongings and magical items around. He took Ozama's snakeskin boots, a magical memento of his lost lover, that subsequently saved his life by neutralizing deadly poisons of the creatures of Myth Drannor's dungeons.

## Jasuga

Jasuka was the capital of the province of the same name in Wa and the second-largest city on the Fochu Peninsula. It was a city that most of the nation of Wa found unimportant and ignored. However, it was notable for the sport of wrestling.

Description
Jasuga was the largest city in its province, but the province itself was Wa's most desolate; the soil was very sandy, which made farming difficult, and it produced very few resources. The citizens of Jasuga survived by the trades of fishing and weaving.

Like many cities of Wa, Jasuga was not welcoming to strangers in its gates, and its people were highly religious.

Geography
Jasuga was located on the west coast of the island of Tsukishima, in the northern portion of the Fochu Peninsula and at the end of the Hisano Road from Fochu. The city sat on the Nanaichi River. East of the city was the Jufosu Range. A trail led north to the village of Sukyo.

Government
The daimyo of the city in Wa Year 1775 (1357 DR) was Nobu Subari. He was not a very successful daimyo, but he was made a noble to fulfill a promise made to his mother by the shogun. He was an avid athletics fan who promoted the sport of wrestling in his city.

Defenses
Jasuga maintained a small militia composed primarily of bushi. The city itself was surrounded by a  stone wall with guards posted on the wall every . The guards on the walls were armed with chu-ko-nu and leaf-headed arrows and wore whistles around their necks with which to summon reinforcements. Guards on foot were armed with katanas.

Trade
The city had a suitable amount of trade with other cities of Wa, and just about anything found in other Kara-Turan cities could also be found there, sold mainly from a central market, but these goods tended to cost about double what they would in other places.

To enter the city required the proving of one's faith and the purchase of a visitor's pass at a cost of 1 yuan per person.

History
Jasuga hosted an annual wrestling competition, held on the holiday of the Ox Festival, but the citizens trained for the sport throughout the whole year.

Notable Locations
 Koi Temple
 Kowa Arena
 Rai Inn
 Sen Inn
 Sugi Inn

Inhabitants
 Hiroyasu Ohta, a master craftsman of katana blades.

## Jaluster's Orizon

Jaluster's Orizon was an ancient arcane spellbook penned by the wizard Jaluster. This tome was carried by Jaluster at the time of his death. It contained five spells ranging from common to unique or rare.

Description
Jaluster's Orizon was a portly tome. Its embossed and painted cowhide was stretched over wooden boards bordered with iron. Each of its twelve pages was a thin piece of slate covered in stretched vellum. The vellum was clamped with hammered electrum. The first five pages held one spell apiece, but the last seven pages were blank.

This book was guarded by a rare creature called a tome guardian.

Powers
Jaluster's Orizon contained the following spells:
Quimby's enchanting gourmet • waves of weariness • rope trick • feign death• Adelimer's aural augmenter

## Jasmarim Shadow

Jasmarim Shadow was a legendary brand of wine created by a long-gone moon elf vintner of the Vintner's Keep. The Jasmarim wine was highly sought after in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Jasmarim Shadow was an extremely rare, red wine.

History
In late 15 century DR, Jasmarim Shadow went for a steep sum of money and was out of production for centuries. All Jasmarim came from one moon elf winemaker who knew how to do only one thing and did it well. He produced his wines from his Vitner's Keep, at the southern border of Lurkwood. His wine was made in limited quantities and small batches, and was reserved for the most exclusive galas and events.

A cook from Luskan's own Cutlass inn, circa the, was searching for a bottle of the exquisite libation to experiment with to improve in his culinary skills and to win cooking competitions. He hired the Champion of the Burning Dawn and their companion to retrieve some of the legendary wine from the Vintner's Keep.

## Jarrlak

A jarrlak was a type of demon found within the Abyss.  Jarrlaks were known both for their intelligence and their sadism, and often toyed with their victims, trapping them in blocks of ice.  Jarrlaks often aligned themselves with various demon lords in an effort to acquire power, but they just as often struck out on their own to attempt to establish control over areas of heavy snow and cold.
Abilities
Jarrlaks were able to both entomb their enemies in ice as well as raise solid walls of ice.
Combat
Jarrlaks attempted to handle enemies one by one, preferring ideally to freeze each one before moving on to the next.

## Jarlmoot

Jarlmoot was a landmark in Icewind Dale, serving as the former seat of power for the frost giant jarls in the region.

Description
The site comprised seven giant-sized stone thrones, arranged in a circle facing one another. There was one throne for each of the frost giant's jarls, one of which was claimed by Jarl of Jarls, Reggaryarva.

Hidden beneath Reggaryarva's throne was an underground hallway that featured six braziers inscribed with Jotun runes. By lighting each of the runes with its required ingredient, a person could gain access to a grand chest holding the giant's treasure.

The six Jotun runes were dod, fjell, krig, liv, vind, and wyrm.

A poem inscribed upon the wall offered clues on how to correctly lite each of the braziers.

Geography
Jarlmoot was located atop a hill, some ways west of the Shaengarne River.

History
In the, the frost giant Gromin'gorn traveled to Jarlmoot in order to face the frost giant specters and claim its hidden treasure.

Inhabitants
On nights of a full moon, the ghosts of the jarls appeared at their thrones, sharing tales about the feats and exploits they accomplished in life.

That same year, the frost druid Yselm Bloodfang sought to lure a group of adventurers to the ancient site, in the hopes of killing them in the name of Auril.

## Jauslo

Civilar Jauslo was a member of Waterdeep's City Watch who was trusted by the Open Lord, Laeral Silverhand circa 1490s DR.

Personality
Jauslo was a serious, stern, and brutal woman who has committed her life to the service to the City of Splendors. She was mistrusting and cruel towards criminals, on occasion throwing them into the Castle Waterdeep's dungeons, simply forgetting about them and letting them slowly die of starvation and dehydration. She always prioritized her duty over her morals. Still, she was willing to turn a blind eye to the individuals who performed goodly deeds, like freeing slaves.

Activities
Jauslo was in charge of a small group of loyal blades.

History
Civilar Jauslo was a loyal member of the City Watch member entrusted by Open Lord, Laeral Silverhand with the mission of recovery of her favorite pet, a baby griffin who was stolen from of the Lord's mansion by the Xanathar's Thieves' Guild circa 1490s DR. Jauslo captured the halfling thief who unsuccessfully attempted to pick the Open Lord's pockets and after making sure the thief was not the member of Xanathar's Guild, "requested" the halfling's aid. She outfitted the little thief with disguise and pointed the halfling towards Skullport, the underground city from which the Thieves' Guild operated from.

## Jardeth

The Jardeth  family was one of the noble houses of Waterdeep circa the . They were known for providing trained soldiers and guards, including provisioning and garrisoning.

Organization
The patriarch of the family was Ulb Jardeth from at least the, to at least 1372 DR. His consort was Lady Allys and their eldest son was Koris. There were forty-one noble members of the clan as of 1372 DR.

Activities
The family business was in training professional soldiers and providing logistic support for armed militias. They were one of four families that sponsored a trade mission to Maztica that eventually became the colony of New Waterdeep. House Jardeth was responsible for the defense of the colony, conducting drills, and recruiting and training new guards.

Base of Operations

The Jardeth family headquarters was a walled compound of two-story buildings surrounding some gardens located in the North Ward of Waterdeep. The west side of the estate fronted on Whaelgond Way overlooking the intersection with Delzorin Street. The somewhat triangular grounds faced Saerdoun Street to the northeast and an alley to the southeast. Directly across Saerdoun Street was the Gralhund family villa.

The Jardeths also has significant holdings and a garrison of troops in New Waterdeep.

History
The Jardeths were of Chondathan descent and worshiped Helm. They were ennobled in the, along with many of their fellow merchant gentry families. As of 1372 DR, there were forty-one living members.

In the, the Jardeths were part of a flotilla of twelve ships that attempted to establish trade at Helmsport in Maztica, but the Lord-Governor of New Amn turned them away, citing limits on transoceanic trade that had been put in place by the Council of Six. The trade armada eventually landed on Maztapan Island and in the space of two months established the New Waterdeep colony.

Members
 Ulb Jardeth: Patriarch in the mid–14 century.
 Allys Jardeth: Consort of Ulb.
 Koris Jardeth: Eldest son and heir of Ulb and Allys.
 Urlymen Jardeth: An estranged family member in the 13 century, his mansion on Ilzantul Street eventually became part of Mrayvrens' Bountiful Rest two centuries later.

## Jaffu

Jaffu was one of the kobolds from Undermountain that raided streets of Waterdeep mid–14 century DR.

Personality
Jaffu and his mate were not aggressive towards adventurers as long as they did not harm the kobold couple, unlike the rest of their tribe.

Abilities
According to the kobold's mate, Jaffu was a good archer.

History
Jaffu and his fellow kobolds were driven from their homes and forced to relocate to the Dungeon Level of Undermountain in 1370 DR. From there, the kobolds started raiding and thieving Waterdeep, which attracted the attention of the Lords of Waterdeep. Khelben Arunsun sent a hero to travel to the dungeon and stop the kobold crime-spree. The hero came across Jaffu in the sewers of the Dungeon Level, where he was tasked the kobolds' leader with the watch while his mate was guarding the looted goods one floor above. Unlike his kin, Jaffu knew that the Lords of the City of Splendors would send someone to stop the raids sooner or later and asked the lone hero to spare him and his mate.

## Janros

Captain Janros was a wood elf officer in the Neverwinter Guard who served his home city in the late 15 century DR.

Relationships
He worked with a number of skilled members of the Guard, including Sergeant Creed, Scout Gaelen, and Private Quickfoot.

History
Janros was a veteran soldier.

Following the Siege of Neverwinter in the, Captain Janros was tasked with defending Neverwinter against the orcish occupiers of the Many-Arrows kingdom under commander Vansi Bloodscar. He led the guardsmen in first containing the orcs and then reclaiming the River District from them. He had soldiers guard the gates and man the walls, and barricaded off parts of the ruined district so they could protect the other districts, but this left the Guard's numbers stretched thin. Thus, Janros regularly recruited adventurers to target important orc leaders and disrupt their war effort.

During that assignment, Sergeant Creed asked an adventurer to find lost dispatches from slain Neverwintan couriers and complete their delivery to Janros. From these, Janros managed to uncover the whereabouts of a holdout Nashers cell led by Karr Nevrim, who colluded with the orcs. Hence, to prevent him undermining the city's security, Janros dispatched the adventurer to invade their sewer base and slay Karr.

Afterward, Janros worked with the same adventurer to check the barricades and district walls to ensure the orcs were not breaking through, recover their stolen supply crates, and eliminate key warband leaders within the Many-Arrows hierarchy, such as their taskmasters and Kartok Paingiver, who'd fortified the old Merchant Guild Hall. Meanwhile, he accepted the Proudfist clan's defection and had half-orc adventurers target spellscarred orcs.

With the Guard and the city secured and the orcs weakened, Janros was ready to strike back. He asked the adventurer to go behind orcish lines to meet Private Quickfoot and Scout Gaelen, who would give them more missions striking the orcs' core. Finally, a team of adventurers penetrated the Cloak Tower, so they could defeat Vansi Bloodscar, and her lieutenants Makred the Foul and Throg the Ravenous, finally ending the war.

## Jarm

Jarm was an evil Netherese arcanist who excelled at spell duels.

Description
This inventor was a methodical and efficient killer; whenever he faced competition, he would make sure that they disappeared. Even mages who were just passing through his hometown of Earsome would find themselves on their deathbed.

Relationships
Jarm had many complicated relationships. In spite of his general lean towards evil, he would put himself on the line against the invasion of his hometown by Hidden Forest orcs. One of his nemeses was Shastin, a good priest of Mystryl, but, unlike his other opponents, he didn't have the resolve to face her. Another one of his enemies was the skilled arcanist, Volhm, a creator of powerful evocation spells.

History
Jarm was born in Earsome in . The evil arcanist invented an array of different spells, starting with mount. After defending his home, and taking out his enemies, he sought out Volhm's spell, lightning bolt, and the two engaged in a spell duel to the death in . Even though he was facing a senior arcanist who specialized in evocation, the masterful Jarm succeeded, and eliminated Volhm. After his victory, Volhm's chaotic students, in rage, rushed Jarm. Jarm, however, killed each of them, one by one.

Years later, he focused on spell development, and invented magic jar, summon swarm, spectral hand, and irritation. Jarm passed away in . Strangely, Jarm was recorded to have invented legend lore in, several years after his death.It is never explained how it was possible that Jarm invented legend lore eleven years after his death. It is possible that one of the dates is wrong, either the spell invention date or the date of his death.

## Javen Tarmikos

Javen Tarmikos was the paladin of Helm who founded and led the Order of the Gilded Eye in the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Javen believed that evil forces should be actively sought and destroyed before they have any opportunity to inflict harm upon others.

As zealous as he was, Javen was not above aligning himself with what he perceived as a lesser evil, in order to defeat something greater and potentially more harmful to the Realms.

Abilities
Javen focused his holy skills in manner that best allowed him to protect others and smite down evil beings.

Relationships
He had a strong ally in Sir Banagar, a fellow devout knight of Helm.

History
For some time Javen served as a member of the Order of the Gauntlet, the group of holy warriors who dedicated themselves to fighting evil along the Sword Coast and within the North. However he became deeply troubled following the invasion of Helm's Hold by Ashmadai and the Prophet Rohini, a succubus who masqueraded as a devoted oracle of Helm. While Javen helped liberate his home city during the Siege of Helm's Hold, circa the, the atrocities he witnessed left him increasingly disillusioned with the Order.

In either Nightal 1486 or Hammer 1487 DR, Javen was meditating at the Cathedral of Helm when he received a vision upon Helm's holy symbol within the church: the unblinking eye of Helm began to weep golden tears. His divine vision was soon followed by others that presented themselves to Javen's fellow clergy members. Inspired by the return of the Watcher, Javen formed Order of the Gilded Eye, holy warriors of Helm that would protect the Realms where the Order of the Gauntlet previously failed.

Circa 1487 DR, Javen and a band of Gilded Eye knights ventured out to destroy the last remnants of the Order of the Burning Dawn adventuring guild. Javen believed that one of their members Soronil Noonshadow was an agent of the balor Belaphoss, and thus the entire guild should be eradicated.

## Jake's Encyclopedia of Eels

Jake's Encyclopedia of Eels was a single self-published tome of knowledge about eels, available in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Jake's Encyclopedia was a shabbily cobbled-together book, loosely bound and filled with unevenly sized pages. The book was written in a similarly chaotic uneven script. The ink used was just as random as the rest of the book; seemingly, the author used whatever ink he could find.

Content
The author's foreword was written as a direct address to the grand library of Candlekeep, as a response to it turning down the author's book.

The book started by arguing that giant lightning eels only look like eels, such as giant morays, but in reality were not closely related to them, on account of their differing head shapes and bone structures.

The next part of the book talked about how abundant eels were across the Realms and that they could be easily caught everywhere: in Neverwinter, Elturel, Calimport, and many other places. The author shared folklore about the origins of these slippery creatures. Some claimed eels were merely fish who learned to swim, some said that eels were baby leviathans, and some even accused them of being spies for the Underdark. However, the author was convinced that all eels bred in the Sea of Moonshae and traveled all over Faerûn from there.

Following this bold claim, the author went on rambling about the scholars of Candlekeep refusing to finance his research on the matter. The book continued this tirade for a few pages until it went back to the topic of eels.

History
In 1492 DR, a copy of Jake's Encyclopedia of Eels could be found in one of the suites of the Waukeen's Rest in the Western Heartlands. Judging by the book's contents, the author, Jake, applied for sponsorship for his eel research with the scholars of Candlekeep sometime before 1492 DR.

## Jarnon

Jarnon was a member of the Society of Stalwart Adventurers from Suzail, active in mid-to-late 14 century DR.

History
In 1362 DR, Jarnon could often be found in the Society of Stalwart Adventurers' library. There, he participated in the reenactments of famous Cormyrian historic battles, using enchanted miniature infantry and monsters. These reenactments were a sort of a war game with several strict rules. During one such match, Jarnon unleashed a tiny lead red dragon to smite his opponent. This act was called a foul by his opponent, and the little dragon's flames damaged the guild's expensive Shou carpets. This event happened shortly before everyone present in the library witnessed Ariast's hands being crushed by a mysterious four-armed humanoid, seeming in service of Artus Cimber.

This bow's peculiar construction allowed for an easier chance of landing a critical strike on a target; however, it was hard to handle. The Jaws was also imbued with magic enhancing its damage output, similar to the ordinary +3 enchantment.

## Javuhansa

Javuhansa was a town in the Kuong Kingdom in Malatra.

Geography
Javuhansa lay at the source of the Kunong River, in the high hill country in the central western Kuong Kingdom.Like many locations in the Kuong Kingdom, Javuhansa has not been labelled on any map of Malatra. However, it is known to be on the headwaters (source) of the Kunong River, which, based on descriptions, is most likely the river from the central hill country and joining the Fenghsintzu River. It stood at the northern end of the trade road to the south, which passed through Enlasre south of Javuhansa.

Trade
Gold and ivory collected in the southern jungles were carried north via caravan to Javuhansa in the mid–14 century DR. These goods were sold in its open markets.

More notably, Javuhansa was the best place to get imported herbs, spices, and medicinal plants in all of Malatra. These were expensive and often rare, even magical.

## Jade River

The Jade River, a portion of which was later named the Barren River, was a once-massive waterway that flowed through central Threskel, one of the Old Empires of east Faerûn.

Geography
It began in the western peaks of the Riders to the Sky mountain range and flowed westward, south of the city Mordulkin, before emptying into the Bay of Chessenta.

Description
The green waters of the Jade River rushed down the mountainside before leveling out and flowing lazily across the plains of Threskel.

The river's color originated was caused by both naturally-forming algae and industrial waste caused by dwarven mines in the Riders' mountains.

History
After the landscape of Threskel changed following the events of the Spellplague in the, the Jade River became no more. A massive plateau erupted from the plains, creating a small lake known as the Jade Pond and a much smaller waterway that share the Jade's mouth at the Bay of Chessenta, known as the Barren River.

## Jade Palace of the Necromancers

The Jade Palace of the Necromancers, also called the Jade Palace of Zoraya or simply the Jade Palace, was a prominent structure found in the city of Ysawis within the Grey Jungle of the Cities of the Ancients region of east Zakhara in 1367 DR.

Structure
The Jade Palace was constructed from polished white sandstone. The outer walls of the palace formed a square. One main gate found in the northwest corner, and an accessory gate in the southeast corner, allowed access to the palace. Each of the four outer walls measured  in length.

Northern Court
The northern court was originally designed with defense in mind. This open courtyard stretched along the entire length of the Palace's northern wall. The northern court was filled with well-manicured lawns as of 1367 DR.

Main Gatehouse
The main gatehouse was a two-storied fortification complete with four ballistae located on the western side of the outer north wall. Juju zombies armed with crossbows were stationed here.

Galleries
Vaults lined the walls of the northern court. Most contained simple gardening tools, but some were stocked with weapons and armor.

Riding Court
The packed-dirt of the courtyard running the length of the western outer wall was once used to train the royal horses.

Royal Stables
The stables were located in between the riding court and the western outer wall of the palace. Bizarre steeds such as ghost mounts, skeletal horses, and even a Zakharan roc were housed here and attended by zombie grooms.

Flower Gardens
The gardens were tended by various undead who maintained rows of violets, gardenias, and orchids during all hours. A circular pool was located in the center of the gardens.

Guest Rooms
A multitude of guest rooms and parlors surrounded the flower gardens. Each was decorated with carved jade panels of varying colors from bone white to red and green.

Interior
The throne room was decorated with dark green jade carved panels depicting the conquests of King Kagemni. A pair of massive jade thrones dominated the throne room. Each weighed .

The riches of Ysawis were housed within the Gallery of Antiquities. The treasury was warded from unwanted intruders with symbols of insanity and a cadre of malevolent spectres.

An overgrown mini jungle within the palace, called the King's Garden, served as a morbid undead zoo for Sumulael to monitor is undead monstrous creations.

Activities

Defenses
An elite force of at least 1,000 undead guarded the Jade Palace including undead monsters, ju-ju zombies, and undead jungle giants.

The necromancers Kazerabet and Sumulael added antiscrying and antiteleportation wards in their separate private chambers within the palace. Both necromancers utilized the improved skull watch spell to decorate the palace with magical skull sentries that they could communicate with telepathically. Each necromancer had sixteen such skull watchers.

All of the corridors and halls within the Palace were enchanted with continual light spells, making it extremely difficult to navigate the Palace by stealth. Furthermore, undead servants prowled the halls at all hours of the day carrying out their designated tasks.

An elite core of 300 ju-ju zombies were outfitted with ancient banded mail and shields bearing the ancient symbols of Ysawis–a griffon and vishap. The majority of ju-ju zombies wielded spears or scimitars as weapons, but some carried crossbows or some form of polearm.

A dozen powerful undead jungle giant slaves, previously of the Silent Arrow Clan, served as elite guards.

History
The palace was originally referred to as the Jade Palace of Zoraya long ago when Ysawis still stood as a mighty city.

Rumors & Legends
Princess Zoraya was rumored to be buried with the legendary Talisman of Shajar in a secret mausoleum hidden somewhere within the Jade Palace.

Inhabitants
The undead roaming the Palace would ignore approved visitors unless they turned hostile. Guests caught sneaking around in unauthorized areas would be detained by the undead and interrogated by lieutenants. Punishment for being caught unininvited within the private sections of the palace was meted out by either Sumulael or Kazerabet.

## Jarval Keth

Jarval Keth was a Mulan   explorer. He discovered the symbol marking the entrance to the hidden Imaskari level in Maskana.

After finding an unusual version of the crescent and the fox pictographs in Maskana, Jarval researched these symbols at Gheldaneth, first asking around in the temples, and then in the Great University. He had been seen in the company of a man with a pale, skull-like face; he was captured by Tyaral at around this point.   Jarval had barely managed to escape Sithena Tinzon, Tyaral's chief inquisitor, by boarding a ship.

Jarval managed to blurt out a few clues to some adventurers before Sithena Tinzon found him. He might've survived the fight that almost certainly ensued; if so, only a heal spell could've cured the mental sequels of his torture. ## Jamal temple

The Jamal temple was a small temple of enlightenment found at the Jamal Oasis in 1367 DR.

Description
This cylindrical temple was made from granite that had been polished smooth by sand over time. The temple was crowned with dozens of minarets carved with enlightened proverbs. Though many of these etchings were unreadable from the passage of time, one at the base of the temple read: "How can any man born of earth claim to divine the true purposes of the gods?"

Rumors & Legends
Djinn were rumored to visit the Jamal temple periodically on the darkest nights to debate the carved words. Any humans presenting apples to the djinn, a favorite treat of theirs, were allowed to join the debate. Participants who exhibited great wisdom stood the chance of being rewarded with wishes while those who were crass got slapped in the face.

## Jamal al-Yindannim

Jamal al-Yindannim was a ranking member of the Brotherhood of the True Flame in Zakhara in 1367 DR.

Description
Jamal was tall at . He was handsome and strong and appeared to be in his early twenties. His dark hair was cropped short. He consumed several potions of longevity to maintain his youthful appearance.

Activities
Jamal served as lord of the fortress of Krak al-Niraan, commanding an impressive array of the Brotherhood's forces and maintaining strong control in that region of the Land of Fate.

Possessions
Jamal owned a wand of the sun, ring of genie summoning, sash of fire resistance, and a pair of bracers of defense. His white aba was fire-proofed and was enchanted with deeppockets. Within his robe was stored his material components, spellbook, six smoke spheres, four greek fire projectiles, and potions of extra-healing, glibness, love, and a vial of Starella's aphrodisiac. Jamal also wielded a pair of poisoned jambiyas and poisoned darts.

Abilities
Though still a skilled flame mage, Jamal's advancement in the elemental arts was slowed by his romantic and militaristic pursuits. When he grew bored of his latest romantic conquest, he "retired" her to a special harim where he used the women to create sinister nasnas that were completely loyal to him.

Relationships
In addition to the poor women sentenced to his overflowing harim, Jamal had two wives–Najeeya bint Siddiq and Qumasha al-Arus.

One of Jamal's enchanted rings gave him command over a warmonger genie named Khaizuran.

History
Jamal studied magic at the university in Qadib. He was particularly interested in fire magic. He enjoyed seducing married women of his fellow students while studying at the university. This behavior led to many sorcerous confrontations with irate husbands, but Jamal always came out on top. It was this nasty reputation that first led the Brotherhood of the True Flame to contact him.

Jamal was tasked by the Bonfire to secure a fortress within the Haunted Lands. This was easily accomplished with the help of Khaizuran. The fortress would be renamed Krak al-Niraan, or the "Castle of Fire."

## Jason Wong

Jason Wong is an actor starring in the upcoming Honor Among Thieves movie, due for release in 2023. He is playing Dralas, an undead warrior.

Career
He appeared in The Gentlemen and Silent Witness, and had a minor role in Solo: A Star Wars Story and Spirit Warriors. He was also a voice actor in the video games Total War: Three Kingdoms, 007 Legends, and James Bond 007: Blood Stone.

Wong was reported by Deadline on May 13, 2021, to be playing a character called Drallas or Dralas in the Dungeons & Dragons movie. Various sites expanded on this, repeating "Dralas is a member of the 'Undead,' a group of once-living creatures who have been brought to a horrifying state of 'undeath' through dark magic." This appears to have been copied from an early version of the Deadline article, with the description of the undead lifted from another D&D wiki, but later removed there and the name changed to "Drallas". The reason and the veracity of this is unknown. Earlier, on May 1, he posted a video on Instagram of himself having a full head cast made for an "exciting new project", likely for a mask or effect for his role.

He confirmed his role following the release of the trailer, remarking "Are you READY for Dralas!!" His character's pale appearance supports his undead nature.

## Jamzo Bakadi

Jamzo Bakadi and his brother, Jemzo Bakadi, were prominent thieves from Memnon of Calimshan circa 972 DR.

History
Jamzo and Jemzo grew up on the hot, sweaty streets of Memnon, surviving on crime and thefts. Eventually, they proved to be the finest rogues to have ever been born in the hot Calishite city. They quickly gained a legendary reputation for their skills, cunning, and bravery. The infamy led them to head one of the major crime cartels of Calimshan.

Sometime circa 972 DR, the brothers came up with a plan to pull off the greatest heist of a lifetime. They disappeared in almost complete secrecy, never to be seen again with only the cartel's lieutenant knowing the thieves' brazen plot.

Bakadi brothers set out to rob a great warrior-wizard Sunder Halyndliam of his magical treasures but failed as was evident by their skulls being stored in the mage's crypt underneath Liam's Hold, after it was briefly opened in 1372 DR. The wizard's punishment must've been grand as the skulls showed signs of arcane tinkering and turned into a powerful artifact known as the Bone Brothers Bakadi.

## Jalesh Rudra

The Jalesh Rudra was a holy celebration of the faithful of Silvanus, celebrated on the night of Tarsakh 19.

During the Jalesh Rudra, priests sought out sacred groves, where they painted each other with the symbols of Silvanus, and made an offering of the leaves of oak, ash, and thorn, preferably over running water. Silvanus' messengers, tree or water spirits (such as dryads), would then seek them out; the Master of the Ceremony was to play music for them to dance to. If the celebrants were found worthy, the Oak Father's favor was shown by allowing the priests to commune (carnally) with the messengers; in some cases, magical items or even quests could also be given to the celebrants.

It also served as a coming of age ceremony for the faithful.

## Jaluster

Jaluster was carrying his latest work, Jaluster's Orizon, when he visited Ascalhorn. He attempted to save the city from demons but was torn apart in the process, leaving his final work incomplete. Before he died Jaluster was said to have personally killed three liches and five demons.

## Jabbar

His Deadliness Jabbar was the head of the Purple Lancers, one half of the Company of the Ebon Spur, a military order that fought in the name of the Mad God Cyric during the mid–14 century DR.

Description
The Cyricist commander hid his pale skin behind layers of priestly robes. He had a sly and almost serpentine smile.

Relationships
Jabbar held an acrimonious and ultimately deadly rivalry with the Dark Lord Haroun, commander of the Black Helms. Even when directed by Cyric to fight alongside one another on the same battlefield, neither leader would look upon the other except to engage in bickering and argument.

Activities
Both Jabbar and Haroun felt so much hatred towards the other that even mundane arguments led to nearly fatal acts. When arguing over admission standards for the Company of the Ebon Spur, each commander devised overly-intricate means in order to kill the other.

Possessions
Jabbar bore with him what was the ultimate design for his personal battle standard: a golden scepter crowned the the skull depicted in Cyric's holy symbol. The Dark Lord Haroun wielded a similar scepter, though one topped with an iron-colored starburst, completing the symbol of the Mad God.

History
In the, Jabbar led the Purple Lancers to rendezvous with the Black Helms atop a hill near the Way of the Lion, just beyond the gates of Candlekeep on the Sword Coast. The entire Company of the Black Spur was tasked with besieging the library-fortress and claiming the Cyrinishad for Cyric's church.

Believing that the arrival of the Loviataran Monks of the White Rod was an ill omen, Jabbar stated that the siege should be postponed until the intent of the Maiden of Pain's followers could be ascertained. A heated argument quickly erupted between His Deadliness Jabbar and the Dark Lord Haroun. When the Finder of the Book Malik el Sami dared disagree with Jabbar, the commander quickly tried to slay the filthy merchant with a poisoned needle. Jabbar missed his mark however, and inadvertently struck Haroun. The Dark Lord responded with a single swing of his starburst scepter into the bottom of Jabbar's skull, instantly killing the commander of the Purple Lancers. Both leaders of the company fell over dead atop their mounts.

## Javalar Roaringhorn

Javalar led a group of adventurers during the exploration of the elven castle of El'lahana Raikeil deep within the Moonwood. After discovering and experimenting with the Crown of Joy and Tears, Javalar exited the castle in the moonlight and declared "May none again find the Crown of Joy and Tears unless they enter with the heart of a child and the laugh of a dryad or wish to regain that which they have lost." Intrigued by these events, an avatar of Lurue appeared and took the adventurers on an exhilarating ride through the forest with her Silver Herd. The group chose to name themselves the Knights of the Unicorn in Lurue's honor and pledged their service to the Unicorn Queen.

Following the Time of Troubles, Javalar became a commander of one of the bands of the Order of the Unicorn, one of several smaller bands split from the Knights of the Unicorn with the hopes of doing good deeds over a larger area.

## János Anatall

János Anatall was an assassin from the Barovian village of Vallaki who was under the employ of Esmae Amarantha in the 15 century DR, a woman whose ritualistic plans threatened to permanently trap within the Domains of Dread the lands and people of Phlan that had been sucked into Barovia.

Possessions
János kept a journal hidden under his mattress. This contained details on all the murders he committed for Esmae, a general explanation of the Evening Glory faith, and his many childish attempts at writing romantic poetry.

History
While under the employ of Esmae, János came to live within one of the guardrooms of the ruined Boszórkan Keep. Over time he became enraptured by both her beauty and power, worshiping her from afar. Following Esmae's merging with the vestige Evening Glory, his devotion to her grew stronger and he longed for the day he would die in her service so that he may be reborn as an undead servant.

János did not believe that Tatyana, a woman who Esmae sought to merge with, was as beautiful as her. Yet he still vowed to love Esmae even after she took on Tatyana's likeness.

## Jacinth dragon

Jacinth dragons were exceptionally solitary gem dragons and the rarest of dragon species.

Description
Jacinth dragons were covered in sparkling scales of various shades of flame-orange that seemed to be in constant motion. They were smaller than other dragons, ranging from as little as  in length, to as much as  in length as great wyrms.

Personality
Jacinth dragons were solitary to the extreme, avoiding all other forms of life.

Abilities
Jacinth dragons spoke only their own dialect, but could telepathically communicate with other telepathic or exceptionally intelligent creatures. Because of their relatively small size, their fear aura was less powerful than that of other dragons, but outside of combat, these dragons could use a combination of riddling talk and personal charm to entrance other creatures. They also had the ability to use the shifting colors of their scales to produce a hypnotic effect; on bright, sunny days, their hide reflected light so brightly that it blinded viewers.

Their breathe weapon was a cone of scalding air some  long and  wide at the far end, which set anything easily flammable within the cone on fire. They had superior magical abilities to most other dragons, being able to cast both wizardly and priestly spells. They could also innately blink six times each day.

Combat
Jacinth dragons typically relied on their breath weapons and spells in combat, but would use their claws and fangs in melee as well.

Ecology
Jacinth dragons lived in hot, dry deserts far from any other creatures. They were capable of going for weeks without food or water. They enjoyed their solitude, but were sometimes overly curious of visitors. Otherwise, they were only known to interact with other people when they went searching for their preferred treasure: jacinth. Beyond that rare gem, they kept little other treasures.

Jacinth dragons were the rarest of dragon species, and no more than a dozen at most lived on any given world. The only creatures known to attack them were adventurers, but the hide of a jacinth dragon had never been taken.

## Jakkob

Jakkob was a werehyena inhabiting the city of Yhep along the Lake of Steam during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Activities
Originally from Zakhara, Jakkob was captured by Calishites while he was in hyena form for a private menagerie. He was able to eventually escape and reached the Lake of Steam region where he became the leader of a pack of natural hyenas after slaying their alpha in combat. Jakkob led the pack on raids against the Talu-min tribe, killing many of their herd animals in the process. The Talu-min eventually caught up to, and slew all of the hyenas, but Jakkob was once again able to escape with his life. He fled to Yhep where he led the town's dog on nightly hunts.

## Janesta Leafgrace

Janesta Leafgrace was a wood elfJanesta is described as both a half-elf and as a wood elf in p. 138 of chapter 11. Given that the description of a half elf is given from Raidon's perspective, while the Master of the Yuirwood speaks of her as a wood elf, this article assumes that Raidon's perception of her as a half elf is likely in error, expecting the Master to be more familiar with the features of elves and half-elves both. and Natal Peacethorn's friend.

History
On, Jamesta was part of a group that studied standing stones which showed the path to a causeway. One day they found the mist between the stones dissipating and the path open for the first time. Entering, she and Natal Peacethorn saw the Gate of Stardeep. The day after, their encampment was attacked by Empyrean Knights. As she had gotten up early to examine a cougar's tracks, she was not at the encampment, leaving her as the only survivor. During the attack, she saw Natal dragged out of his home.

She hid during the attack, and made it back to Relkath's Foot, where she told of the attack, with the aid of the Masters of the Yuirwood. Janesta identified the precise location of the Causeway Gate to Stardeep to all present.

## Jathrin

Jathrin was a criminal whose execution gave name to the town of Jathrin's Jump in Turmish.

Description
Jathrin was a highly intelligent and slim human male,  tall. In his undead form, he was seen wearing ripped leather armor and carrying a lit ghostly torch.

History
Long before the late-14 century DR, a criminal named Jathrin was executed via being tossed off a cliff. With time, a smuggling stop grew into a relatively lawless town that took its name from the criminal – Jathrin's Jump.

Circa the, Jathrin's ghost started appearing on the cliff of Jathrin's Jump. The apparition returned to the Prime Material plane to guard loot he stashed away before the execution. His haunting was not to bring justice for his heath, nor caused by a disturbed burial ground. The ghost of Jathrin was guided by hate for all living, whom he blamed for the undead state. He attacked the living in sinister silence targeting lone travelers rather than well-outfitted adventuring bands. At times when he encountered individuals who were able to damage his incorporeal form, Jathrin always fled. His main weapon was his ghostly aging gaze.

## Jander Turbalt

Jander Turbalt was the captain of the ship Morning Bird based in Tharkar in the Utter East in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Jander was fat and craven.

Personality
Jander was cowardly and incompetent. He lost all his last scraps of courage and dignity after the sinking of the Morning Bird and only despair, rage, and shame remained to drive him.

History
Captain Turbalt was hired by a mysterious individual named Belmer Droon to transport himself and his hirelings under the cover of night from Tharkar to Eldrinpar in Doegan. The voyage turned out to be a dangerous one as shortly after sailing the Morning Bird was chased by a grim-looking black ship. The black ship bombarded the Bird with fire pot missiles and terrifying boneballs, making the passengers and the crew fight for their lives. Narrowly escaping the pursuers, thanks to Belmer's skill and magic items, the Morning Bird found itself besieged by Umberlee's ghost ship and its sea zombies.

After appeasing the Bitch Queen, the Morning Bird was caught up with by the Black Dragon, helmed by the infamous captain Orim Redbeard. Belmer Droon managed to convince the pirate captain to leave them alone, even though one of his hirelings, Kurthe Lornar, was getting ready to take over the Bird and pursue Orim Redbeard, blinded with vengeance. Kurthe Lornar himself was killed in a duel with Belmer over the attempted mutiny.

Soon after, the Morning Bird sprouted leaks, likely because of Orim Redbeard's magic, rotting the hull of the ship. The crew and passengers had to evacuate the sinking ship and swim with their belongings to the shores of Doegan. Jander and a big group of his sailors survived the sinking alongside the Sharkers but the captain blamed the loss of his ship upon Belmer and the Sharkers.

Turbalt's resentment over the ship's loss was only exacerbated by the apparent threat of fiends and monsters who roamed the wilderness of Doegan. One by one, all the sailors were killed by the fiends and only the Sharkers and Jander Turbalt remained.

After arriving in Eldrinpar in Doegan, Jander revealed to the paladins from Waterdeep led by Miltiades the true identity of Belmer Droon—the famed assassin Artemis Entreri, hoping they would avenge the loss of his ship. He said he'd been held captive for the last week by the Sharkers. After hearing his betrayal, Entreri calmly drew a small blade and threw it at Turbalt's head, instantly killing him.

## Jalensifer's trident

Jalensifer's trident was a spell developed by Meldryn Jalensifer.

Effects
On casting the spell, one of the caster's spells was transformed into three lower skill spells within his mind. The spells generated had to have been cast over a dozen times at some point by the mage, and the last time had to be within three days of using the trident. They became available for use, however, through a mere act of will, calling for no components or concentration, and even allowing for the spells in question to be unleashed within seconds of each other, though not at the same time as another spell was being cast. The spell to be sacrificed had to be one or two tiers below the power of the trident itself.

Many mages used this spell to give themselves three readily available magic missiles, for instance.

Components
The spell bore verbal components, but also required as material components a tiny metal trident or three-tined fork, and a piece of vellum, or parchment, that had been used once to bear magic. The metal teeth had to be piercing the paper throughout the casting.

History
Meldryn Jalensifer spent a year crafting scrolls of this spell, which he viewed as his most useful, at some point after or during . He then spread them in a whirlwind tour across the Realms sometime between  and .As Jalensifer delivers the scepter of Mystra in 994, and after that, spends at least one year crafting scrolls before his tour, it could have happened no earlier than 995, and no later than 998, when he dies.

## Jabberwock

Jabberwock were illusive dragon-like creatures that could be found in the Feywild.

Description
Since only a small percentage of people who came into contact with jabberwocks survived the experience, and even fewer were unaffected by the creature's confusing burbling, it was difficult to give accurate accounts of them. It was described as a sinewy, dragon-like monster that moved on all four legs as effortlessly as on its hind legs. A jabberwock could reach the length of  with its tail adding additional  to its size.

One eyewitness accord managed to capture one of the more reliable descriptions of a jabberwock. It was reported to have a thick draconic torso, powerful hind legs, and sinuous front paws, each ending with four feathery taloned fingers. Some sages believed that these feathery growths were a type of parasitic moss. Each talon was about  long. Their bodies were covered in scales the color of tarnished golds and browns. They had a long anguiform neck, a massive head, a pair of leathery wings, and a freakishly long tail. Their heads had two large menacing yellow eyes and red pupils, covered by semitransparent eyelids. Jabberwock's orbs glowed in the dark – a blood-chilling sight in a dark forest. The eyes were also the most vulnerable part of the monster.

Abilities
Jabberwock was a fearsome foe. It was immune to to all poisons, fear, charm, and could only be harmed by arms enchanted with vorpal magics. Non vorpal weapons were capable of damaging the creature but could not deal lethal damage. To make them even more dangerous, jabberwock possessed a powerful regenerative ability. However, it could be slowed by successfully slashing into the beast's flesh. The beast's eyes observed the world in truesight that reached  around them. Despite only appearing to be dragons in a loose superficial sense, jabberwock was surrounded by a powerful aura of fear. They also possessed a powerful resistance to most types of magic.

Like many other predators, they had a powerful, if not supernatural, ability to track prey. If a creature was wounded by a jabberwock, it could track the victim with ease for 24 hours.

Behavior
Jabberwock was in a state of constant confusing burbling to itself. These insane murmurings affected any creature that was within earshot, usually within  but reach up to . Those who were unfortunate enough to hear a jabberwock were affected by hallucinations, compelled to run in a random direction, stay stunned and dazed, or attack a random creature within sight, be they a friend or foe. The confusion was much more powerful than the effects of the spell of the same name and lasted as long as the creature remained within the jabberwock's territory. Additionally, those affected lost their ability to clearly communicate with each other as if affected by the babble spell.

Despite its predatory nature, jabberwock was a curious creature that lacked the instinct to hoard treasure, common among true dragons. Another similarity was their ability to fly, but they rarely did so as jabberwock preferred to lay in waiting, hidden by vegetation or among treetops.

Combat
When a jabberwock descended on a target, it focused all of its attacks on that individual until the target was either dead (and consumed), the jabberwock was eliminated, or the target managed to flee using teleportation magic or another method. Unlike other dragons and related beings, jabberwock emitted flaming rays of fire from their eyes or rays of paralyzation magic.

Ecology
A jabberwock was a lone, erratic predator that prowled wild woodlands and abandoned or ruined sites. The creature was fiercely territorial and did not hesitate to slay anyone brave or stupid enough to stray into the jabberwock's territory. Every time a jabberwock was killed, another specimen mysteriously materialized after a period of time between 2 and 24 years within  of the previous jabberwock's body. Their supernatural nature was discerned from the fact that no young jabberwock had ever been witnessed, nor living jabberwock were known to age. Some believed that just like a tarrasque, there existed only one creature, the Jabberwock.

Most living beings were nothing but food to a jabberwock. Druids were the only exception as the beast left the servants of the forest alone. A certain wise sage proposed a theory that a jabberwock was created by the forests, as its defender, a manifestation of a sort of an anti-body. Only the biggest and most ancient untarnished forests were thought to be able to spawn a jabberwock.

## Jaws (Suzail)

The Jaws was the stream that fed into Lake Azoun and after entering a culvert, drained into Suzail's commercial deepwater harbor called the Basin.

Geography
As of the mid-to-late 14 century DR, the Jaws entered the city of Suzail from beyond its northeastern walls, trickled north and expanded into Lake Azoun on the southern side of Castle Obarskyr. From the lake, it hooked back north around the Court Close and moved back south until turning into a culvert on the opposite side of the block where the Winking Eye stood.

By the, the Jaws' length was greatly shortened. The stream's culvert tunnel began between Castle Obarskyr
and Cormyr's Royal Court, with the rest of the stream paved and built over.

Notable Locations
Cormaeril Compound, the estate of the noble House Cormaeril that stood across the Royal Ride from Greatgaunt Estate.
Greatgaunt Estate, the manor of the noble House Greatgaunt, before the 15 century DR – the estate of House Wyvernspur. It stood to the south of Rowanmantle Castle.
The Refuge, the magical refuge monitored by the Royal Magician and created by Magister Maldiglas Turntower. It was hidden nearby the Tower of the Royal Magician, on the southern shore of the Jaws.
Rowanmantle Castle, the home estate of the noble House Rowanmantle stood on the western side of the Jaws as the stream entered Suzail.
Royal Gardens and Castle Obarskyr – the Dragon Throne of Cormyr.
Thistleflame Manor, the fortified gated keep, home of the noble Thistle family of Suzail stood across the Jaws from the Cormaeril Compound.

## Jalith

Jalith was one of the two barmaids of the Copper Coronet, a rowdy tavern in the River District of Athkatla, in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Jalith was a surprisingly cheerful well-endowed young woman. She lived in a run-down and dangerous district of Athkatla that made her utterly fearless in the face of danger, drunkards, or other troubles her occupation brought.

History
By the late 14 century DR, Jalith worked alongside Maerae at the Copper Coronet. Both women were paid exclusively in food and drink. The Copper Coronet worked them hard, and the only time they had to make actual coin was dawn-to-highsun – their allotted sleeping time away from the tavern. As a way of income, Jalith was hired to run errands and perform various tasks during her off time. She did not fear getting down and dirty while performing hired labor and, occasionally, showed impressive acting skills when performing certain tasks disguised as a wealthy lady. Despite her tough life, Jalith showed no interest in cheating or swindling her clients for moral or self-preservation reasons.

## Jasurra Phaerdinzer

## Jastaath

Jastaath was a petty kingdom located in ancient Narfell that predated the Empire of Narfell. Ancient texts claimed that it was the first Nar kingdom to practice demonbinding.

Location
The kingdom was centered on Jastaath Castle, located on Mount Nar.

History
Jastaath was ruled by priest-kings who were said to be the first of the Nars to parley with demons. They were said to have been powerful enough to control the weather.

The kingdom disappeared and their capital was abandoned for unknown reasons, but the reputation of Jastaath's ancient power persisted. As of the late 15 century DR, the ruins of their kingdom were occupied by Branimern Rythil, who sought their ancient demonbinding secrets.

Appendix
References

## Jastaath Castle

Jastaath Castle was the name given to a massive ruined fortress that sat high on Mount Nar in Narfell.

Description
The ruins were immense, and sat atop the western slopes of the mountain above a glacier. They were buried under a perpetual blanket of snow and ice.

History
The castle had been the seat of power for the ancient Nar kingdom of Jastaath. In ancient times, the magic of Jastaath's demonbinders had altered the weather on Mount Nar to make the castle warm and comfortable even in the winter, but after it was abandoned for unknown reasons, the castle fell into ruins that became buried beneath snow and ice.

As of the mid-to-late 14 century DR, no explorers had successfully found the ruins owing to the harsh climate and the dracolich Kryonar, who laired nearby and was known to kill any visitors to the area. The castle's very existence eventually came to be regarded as a local legend.

At some point around or before the, the Red Wizard Branimern Rythil and her minions arrived at the ruins seeking the lost magical knowledge of Jastaath's ancient demonbinders. They destroyed Kryonar and erected a towering stronghold amidst the castle ruins, which was visible from as far away as Bildoobaris. From her stronghold, Branimern spent the next several decades researching and plotting to conquer the surrounding areas. All the while, the local Nars stayed away from the ruin out of fear that Kryonar might still be alive.

Inhabitants
As of the late 15 century DR, the castle ruins were home to Branimern Rythil and her demon and fey minions, including a fearsome fomorian known as Hughan.

Appendix
References
