## Jared Deathstalker

Jared Deathstalker was a cleric of Kelemvor who opposed the ancient Netherese lich Umgatlik in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Jared Deathstalker was clean-shaven, both face and head, and wore dark plate armor adorned with colors and symbols of the Church of Kelemvor.

Possessions
The Kelemvorite cleric wore gauntlets of ogre power and was armed with a large enchanted +1 shield, carried a scroll of the battletide spell, and several curative potions.

History
{{Quote|Evil succubi, dark rituals, and the stink or undeath. We must ensure that Umgatliks evil taint can never be summoned into this world again!'''|Jared Deathstalker}}
In, Jared Deathstalker joined a bumbling scholar from Waterdeep, Savant Gumbril, in an expedition to uncover the location of the shrine of Umgatlik. Gumbril believed Umgatlik to be a lost Netherese god. His accidental discovery reached the ears of the Church of Velsharoon and Vetrixia Nightmaiden secretly infiltrated the expedition, hoping to release the immensely powerful Netherese lich out of his ancient planar prison. The expedition discovered the ancient tomb in the Crab's Claw Coast, and Jared Deathstalker was there, ensuring the lich's continued imprisonment.

Vetrixia hoped to find an ally in the so-called Hero of Daggerford, offering him arcane power and ancient treasures. It was not recorded if the Hero agreed to the proposition, but that did not stop Vetrixia from returning to Gumbril and confronting Jared.

Within the crypt, Vetrixia Nightmaiden attempted to manipulate Savant Gumbril into doing her bidding through promises of riches and academic success. Dimwitted Gumbril was torn between dreams of a grand academic future and Jared Deathstalker's calls for the scholar's common sense. The lich could be unleashed through a simple ritual using undead bones and a special scepter upon the tomb's altar. Jared was attempting to discourage outsiders' from tampering with Umgatlik's shrine peacefully but would eagerly take up blade to slay those attempting to unleash the creature on Toril. If a Hero were to oppose the lich's return or brave enough to venture into Umgatlik's prison plane to slay Jared Deathstalker was ready to aid in the task.

## Jack's Folly

Jack's Folly was a pair of old and weathered leather boots that once belonged to a would-be treasure seeker named Jack Corvet.

Powers
The boots' humble appearance hid their magic well. Once per day upon command, Jack's Folly unleashed the enlarge person spell.

History
Sometime before the, Jack Corvet attempted a daring robbery of a cloud giant. He claimed the giant's golden chicken, but his stealth failed the adventurer, and the giant quickly gained on Jack. The adventurer whispered a wish upon a lucky ring inherited from his grandmother. To everyone's surprise, Jack grew in size, big enough to be able to punch the giant out and flee with the precious poultry treasure. When the chicken laid a golden egg, Jack sold it and, with the money, replaced his old raggedy pair of boots.

Later, Jack Corvet attempted the robbery again, but the "magic" ring did nothing this time. Jack did not survive the burglary. Such was the story of Jack's Folly as true magic was hidden within his old boots. By 1374 DR, the boots found their way into the collection of Mintassan the Magnificent, proprietor of Mintassan's Mysteries in Westgate's Market Triangle.

## Jack Corvet

Jack Corvet was an ill-fated adventurer who met his doom sometime before the late 14 century DR.

History
Jack Corvet took upon himself the adventuring career after receiving a lucky ring from his grandmother. Sometime before the, Jack attempted to steal golden chicken from a cloud giant. Jack overestimated his skills and he was caught in the act.  The adventurer whispered a plea upon his lucky ring and to everyone's surprise, Jack grew in size, big enough to be able to punch the giant out.

When the chicken laid a golden egg, Jack sold it and, with the money, replaced his old raggedy pair of boots. Later, Jack Corvet attempted the robbery once again, but the "magic" ring failed to enlarge the man. Jack was killed during that adventure and his old discarded pair of boots became known as Jack's Folly.

## Jannaxil Serpentil

Jannaxil Serpentil was a shopkeeper inhabiting Waterdeep during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Jannaxil was a short and plumpish man, whose black hairs were oiled and smoothed into place. He wore a sober scholar robe, open over finely tailored dark clothes, in an attempt to show that he was both a successful merchant and a learned person.

He was one of Waterdeep's best fences. If any printed work was stolen in the city, he usually knew about it by the next day.

Personality
Jannaxil was as greedy and cowardly man who drove a hard bargain. Most people considered him nasty or unfriendly, but he knew how to be charming if the situation demanded it, and acted obsequiously in the presence of nobility.

He never confronted anyone trying to steal from him directly—as magics prevented them from leaving the shop, he would simply wait until they paid or set down their spoils.

The old man had running disputes with several scholars of the city. They took the form of insults and slander, malicious gifts and offending letters, and even hiring bards to spread discrediting songs in the town's taverns.

Finally, Jannaxil loved good books. He was known to pay a hefty sum for nicely illuminated tomes, old maps, or collections of well-illustrated female figures.

Abilities
Jannaxil was known to dabble in the Art, but the extent of his powers were unclear. Some pretended that he lived for far longer than his looks led one to believe, while others said he was able to summon baatezu and had various monsters do his bidding.

Jannaxil and his shop were protected by hundreds of magical spells, from glyphs attacking would-be thieves to magical alarms warning him if someone entered uninvited or tried to leave without paying for his wares.

Possessions
Jannaxil owned more books than most people would ever see in their life. He used various glowing globes to illuminate the interior of his boutique.

He possessed a wide variety of magical items and spellbooks that he gathered over the years and carried on his person. These included a bag of dust of tracelessness, a brooch of shielding, a hat of disguise, a necklace of adaptation, a periapt of proof against poison, a pair of eyes of minute seeing, a pair of gauntlets of ogre power and gauntlets of swimming and climbing, several jars of Keoghtom's ointment, a wand of paralyzation, an uncounted collection of Quaal's feather tokens, and a black, slim book which Volothamp Geddarm believed to be a tome guardian. He possessed even more items than just these, though they were said to be hidden somewhere underground in Waterdeep.

Activities
Jannaxil owned Serpentil Books & Folios, a book shop located in Waterdeep's Dock Ward on the eastern side of Book Street just south of Black Wagon Alley. For a steep fee, Jannaxil would perform book searches for customers trying to locate rare publications.

In addition to running his shop, Jannaxil was also a fence for stolen maps, scrolls, charts, and books. He also dealt in the sale of animals and slaves.

Regularly buying account papers and letters from malicious or careless customers, Jannaxil used them to blackmail his fellow Waterdeep residents.

History
Jannaxil was born in rural Amn. He learned early on in life how to craft potions of longevity and killed the man from whom he learned this from.

During the, Jannaxil was attacked by Elaith Craulnober, leaving him with an intense hatred for elves.

Rumors & Legends
Secret tunnels
According to tavern talks, Jannaxil built hidden tunnels where he hid caches of powerful magic protected by monsters, including copies of every useful spells found in the books he sold over the years.

Appendix
See Also
 Dwitt and Jym Serpentil, who ran Serpentil Books & Folios during the 15 century DR.

## Jack

Jack was a mysterious Red Wizard agent from the eastern nation of Thay, active in the 15 century DR.

Abilities
Jack was a powerful spellcaster who used the school of necromancy in service of the Red Wizards. He possessed magics that temporarily made him invulnerable to magic and melee attacks. His favorite offensive magics included deadly black vapors and death bolts. Jack commanded a small force of boneshards, skeletons, blazing skeletons, chillborns, and zombies.

History
Following the years of Spellplague sowing chaos in the city of Neverwinter, Jack was found performing his foul necromantic experiments in the Neverdeath graveyard of Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR. The mad Red Wizard's laboratory was hidden within the ruins of ancient Illefarn, the fact that became known to Lord Never after Jack's correspondence was recovered by a band of heroes after venturing into the undead-infested Neverdeath. A group of heroes was soon after dispatched to Jack's laboratory by Doomguide Volahk who feared yet another grave plot of Thay.

## Jarull Cartcoster

Jarull Cartcoster was a young man of Yhaunn who fell afoul of a Sharran plot in 1373 DR.

Description
Jarull was a large, powerfully built man. His orcish ancestry granted him heavier features and a grayish cast to his skin.

Relationships
Jarull's paternal grandmother was an orc, and his half-orc father had died early in his life on a foolish adventure. His only remaining family member was his mother Hane, who was perpetually worried that her son would also meet a poor end. He paid no heed to her worries.

He met Keph Thingoleir as a teenager, and the two of them became inseparable friends who caroused and brawled across the city.

History
In Kythorn of the Year of Rogue Dragons, 1373 DR he voluntarily joined the secretive Temple of Shar, only to be held prisoner by Variance Amatick, who tortured him and denied him food and water. In this time, Variance stole his identity and used his persona to manipulate Keph into joining the cult and falling under her influence.

While he was missing from home, his mother had Keph arrested and interrogated on suspicion of his involvement, and had Strasus Thingoleir attempt to magically divine his location, to no effect.

When Keph refused to have any more dealings with the cult, Variance revealed the real Jarull and used him as a hostage to ensure Keph would continue serving her interests.

Jarull was finally rescued by Guard Captain Manas after two tendays, physically and mentally broken. He was then sent to the temple of Ilmater for treatment and recovery.

## Jarnathan

Jarnathan was an aarakocra and one of the ten members that served on the Absolution Council, the ruling body of the Revel's End prison and the group that oversaw the fates of the prison's inmates on behalf of the Lords' Alliance, during the late 15 century DR.It is unknown which settlement Jarnathan represents. Available options are Amphail, Longsaddle, Mirabar, Mithral Hall, Silverymoon, Waterdeep, and Yartar.

Personality
As an aarakocra, he was thought to be the councilor most likely to support pardoning and releasing prisoners. He had a distinguished air.

Description
He had brown feathers and wore red robes.

History

In the late 1490s DR, the Absolution Council—Jarnathan, Kriv Norixius, Voss Anderton, and Jil Torbo—were to meet in Revel's End to consider a pardon for Edgin Darvis and Holga Kilgore for the crimes of skulduggery and grand larceny. However, a storm delayed Jarnathan's journey so the council started without him, but Edgin kept inquiring about Jarnathan's absence, when he'd arrive, and how unfortunate it was that he'd miss out on his story. He hoped that Jarnathan would free them. And he would, just not in the way that anyone expected. When Jarnathan entered, Edgin enacted his 'plan', with him and Holga fighting off the guards, grabbing the aarakocra, and jumping through the window, forcing him to fly them to ice below. Jarnathan was knocked out by the hard landing, but survived.

More than a year later, the same council met to consider Forge Fitzwilliam for pardon, but were wise to the same tricks and refused him. Forge also grabbed Jarnathan and tried to jump through the window, only to find it bricked up behind a curtain, stunning both himself and the poor aarakocra.

Appendix
Background
Jarnathan is played by actor Clayton Grover.

A prop or costume of Jarnathan appeared at the Tavern display promoting the Dungeons & Dragons: Honor Among Thieves movie at the San Diego Comic-Con 2022, as seen here.

As described in Journey Through the Jungle, Jaw accompanied an unnamed traveler through the enigmatic and perilous jungles of Chult. Their foray into the wilds were cut short however, when they heard the call of, and were rapidly beset by the legendary monster known as Uluu Thalongh. The pair attempted to flee by climbing a pair of trees, but were ultimately too slow. Silent in its assault, and leaving no trace of its movement, Uluu Thalongh wrapped itself around Jaw's leg, and claimed her as its latest victim. Jaw's patron was left alive, thus allowing her story to be immortalized in the pages of Journey.

## Jar

Jar was a general term for a style of cylindrical container in the Realms that came in a variety of sizes.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue for 8 copper pieces to 3 gold pieces depending on the size.

Usages
 Jars made of ceramic were used across Faerûn as receptacles to store common table wine. Notably, they were known for a satisfying uncorking sound that grew more pleasing the emptier the jar was. Large ceramic jars were used in wine-making in Muluk, a city in Zakhara.
 Though jars of all types were certainly common household items in the Realms, wizards often used them to store specific ingredients or material components.
 Through the use of special rituals and alchemical preservative fluids, necromancers could use glass jars to preserve the mind of a creature into a form of undead known as a brain in a jar.
 Wax-sealed jars could be used to store pickled foods and preserves, such as honeyed figs, whole lemons, and eggs.

Varieties
 Amphora, a type of jar typically used to hold wine.
 Canopic jar, a type of jar used to store preserved internal organs.

Notable Jars
 Jar of preserving, enchanted glass jars that could preserve any type of material indefinitely.

Notable Users
 Jars were favored by wizards, who often had dozens within their laboratories.

## Jaccob

Jaccob was a halfling smuggler and thief turned tourist guide in the Ten Towns of Icewind Dale in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Jaccob was a  tall hin who weighed . He had brown hair and hazel eyes.

Abilities
The halfling used clubs, daggers, and slings in battle. He was proficient in appraisal, rope use, and could write and read in Common.

Possessions
Jaccob's possessions included lock picks, charcoal, aniseed, a hand lamp, hollow boots, a false scabbard, a mouse cart, three pet mice, and an enchanted longtooth dagger.

History
Jaccob made a name for himself working as a smuggler to and from the northern city of Luskan. His career ended in earning a large bounty on his head, forcing the halfling to flee north to the frigid Ten Towns. Some claimed he had been the second halfling to ever visit the Ten Towns. Jaccob found a fleeting temporary home while apprenticing under Henry Coldwater as a tourist guide at the Breakneck Resort. Henry saw potential and initiative in the newcomer. However, Breakneck Resort proved its name to the amateur skier. Only the most proficient skiers and orcs returned from the slopes unharmed. A short time later, Henry Coldwater hosted a eulogy for the dearly departed Jaccob the halfling.

## Jacinth Moonspring

Jacinth "Jackie" Moonspring was the Society Editor who contributed to the Ravens Bluff Trumpeter broadsheet of Ravens Bluff in the late 14 century DR.

History
Jacinth Moonspring worked for the editor-in-chief Fred Faber. The new editor provided the readers of the Trumpeter with a monthly scoop, gossip, and news from the Living City's cream of the crop. In the very first column, Jackie reported on the Lord Mayor Charles Oliver O'Kane and his love triangle that originated several years prior. Apart from the politician, the love triangle included the beauty named Allandra Mystallan and a wildmage of the Bluff, Belanor Fenmarel, during the mage's announcement of a mayoral electoral campaign. Reportedly, Allandra was seen holding hands with the Lord Mayor just a month later at the Ravens Bluff Bazaar. Just four months later, Jacinth reported on the tragedy that shook the gala that took place during the Winter Festival. The gala was dedicated to the wedding of Lord Charles Blacktree IV and Lady Katherine Marie Moorland, during which Jacinth Moonspring was sitting in the front row. The gala was interrupted by an unnamed guardsman who rushed into the hall with the dire news of a Zhentish attack on Ravens Bluff. Three months later, Jacinth's column reported on Don Juan Montery, a notorious Golden Rooster knight philanderer.

## Jassal

Jassal was an expensive perfume with peculiar properties found in the Realms.

Description
Jassal appeared as a translucent blue-green alcohol. It exuded the smell of ripe cherries, and when applied briefly chilled the skin (from alcohol evaporation) before turning clear and imparting a subtle, wafting cherry scent to the wearer for about six hours.

Usages
In addition to its use as a pleasant perfume, jassal found an appreciation with the church of Loviatar, who came to use it in many rituals. They discovered that if someone was drenched in jassal and then lashed with a whip, it would create an intense burning sensation across the body and heal all minor wounds, scratches, and bruises on their person. It would also produce a loud cracking sound on impact, and exuded a strong burst of cherry scent even if the perfume had already faded to almost nothing.

Preparation
The recipe for jassal was believed to involve alcohol, cherries, and more secret ingredients, but it actually consisted of alcohol and the distilled roots of thistles, nettles, and three flowering weeds.

## Jagress Dawnlion

Jagress Dawnlion was a human Lathanderite priest that lived and died in southern Unther sometime before the Spellplague devastated the region.It is not specified in the adventure whether the ruins being explored belonged to Unther or Tymanchebar. However, owing to the adventure only making note of ruins of "the old Untheric Empire" and the presence of a Lathanderite sarcophagus, it stands to reason the ruins were from Unther.

History
Jagress was buried wearing white robes, in a stone sarcophagus with their name etched into the lid, within the mausoleum of a burial site. Their sarcophagus was designed with a false bottom to hide their belongings within it, which included a number of gold pieces, a +1 amulet of mental resolution, and a +1 symbol of turning.

In the late 15 century DR, when the surrounding area was part of what was known as Tymanther, the mausoleum where Jagress rested was desecrated by a human necromancer and later a member of the Gem Assayers Union named Melnar. The former took Jagress's skull and enchanted it with Skull Watch to watch over the mausoleum. Later, a group of adventurers sent by Dirnth of the Gem Assayers Union to find Melnar would inspect Jagress's sarcophagus, finding their body had long since been destroyed but discovering the hidden compartment.

## Javilarhh Snowsword II

King Javilarhh Snowsword II was a king of Delimbiyran in the early 7 century DR, who ruled during the collapse of Phalorm.

Relationships
Javilarhh's ancestor was King Javilarhh Snowsword I, and his descendent was King Javilarhh Snowsword III.

History
Javilarhh ruled as the king of Delimbiyran and as one of the three kings of Phalorm in the later years of the realm. In the, the Realm of Three Crowns collapsed, and the next year Javilarhh reformed Delimbiyran into the Kingdom of Man to succeed it. Under his leadership, Delimbiyran took control of the land that the elves and dwarves of Ardeep and Dardath had abandoned, and offered protective rulership for the halflings and gnomes of Secomber and Dolblunde. Well aware of the threat that orc raids posed, Javilarhh built a great many castles along the eastern border of the High Moor to guard against invasion and initiated the Endless Battle, an aggressive series of military campaigns that had the armies of Delimbiyran strike at the orc populations of the Sword Mountains and southern High Moor.

## Javilarhh Snowsword I

Relationships
Javilarhh's descendant was King Javilarhh Snowsword II.

History
In the, the human realm of Elembar was overrun by an orcish horde that ultimately foundered on the House of Stone. Although the capital of Delimbiyran and the surrounding land survived, northern and middle Elembar was ruined and the realm was reformed as Delimbiyran with Javilarhh as the first king. In the, King Javilarhh joined King Ruardh Lightshiver of Ardeep and King Torghatar of Dardath as one of the three kings of Phalorm at the Council of Axe and Arrow, joining together to mutually benefit their people.

During the invasion of the Howltusk Horde in the, the orcs were defeated at the House of Stone, though King Javilarhh died in the battle.

## Jarbo

A jarbo was a desert-dwelling rodent known for its speed and its preternatural ability to sense the presence of water.

Description
Jarbos were characterized by their large hind legs, which could propel their furry bodies forward at tremendous speeds. Their forelegs were stubby and their tails were long to provide balance. Different subspecies could vary in appearance, notably in their number of toes, the size of their ears, and/or whether there was fur on their tails. While their coats were generally a sandy color to help them camouflage in desert terrain, their coloration also varied depending on the environment. A mature jarbo grew to a height of about, but could squeeze into a much smaller space.

Behavior
While timid and skittish, jarbos were loyal companions if they recognized someone as a master or friend.

Jarbos were drawn to water, and even a small amount could be used as a lure for them.

Abilities
Jarbos were known to be blindingly fast.

They possessed a psionically enhanced sense of smell that allowed them to detect water across great distances regardless of the direction of the wind. For example, they could sense even a spoonful of water from, a barrelful from, and an oasis from across an entire region. This enhanced sense also helped them to detect edible and fluid-rich plants and insects.

Combat
While their teeth were quite sharp, jarbos were not particularly dangerous, especially because their first instinct was to flee from any creature bigger than them. The only exceptions to this were either if a tame jarbo sensed that its master was in danger or if a jarbo was driven mad by sickness: in rare instances, a jarbo came down with a rabies-like disease that caused them to foam at the mouth and to attack anything in sight, often fighting to the death. Creatures bitten by such a jarbo were at risk of becoming seriously ill.

Ecology

Jarbos could be found in the arctic, subarctic, and temperate deserts of the the North, such as in Anauroch. They were preyed upon by all manner of predators in their native deserts, and served as a key link in many food chains. To avoid these predators, jarbos were migratory, using their speed to outpace any pursuers. They traveled in colonies, known as "flocks", that migrated from one source of water to the next, generally by night. When a flock arrived at a new water source, they would locate a dry area to construct dozens of nests by digging out long, winding tunnels barely  that led to burrows that were  across. When predators inevitably caught up after a few days, the jarbos would leave that night to flee to a new source of water.

While their migrations were dictated by water, jarbos actually needed very little water to survive: an adult could thrive on little more than  of water per day, and could survive almost a tenday with no water at all. This was in part because their diet—consisting of seeds, grass, and insects—provided them with enough water to survive.

Despite the occasional jarbo being overcome by the insanity-inducing "foaming disease", jarbos were generally not prone to disease.

Uses
The right buyer might pay as much as one gold piece for a jarbo pelt in good condition. These were occasionally used for ritualistic purposes, such as in clothing or ceremonial furniture, however their skins were generally too small and thin to be valuable to fur traders.

## Jarkus

Jarkus was a half-elf thief and scout in the late 14 century DR who died while on an expedition in the Shining Sea.

History
In the, sometime around the month of Flamerule, a group of eccentric entertainers, entrepreneurs, and scientists embarked upon a colonizing expedition to an island they discovered in the Shining Sea off the coast of Chult, which they dubbed the "Forgotten Island". Jarkus was among the members of this group, alongside his thief wife Periscot. Led by a charismatic Waukeenar priest named Devlin, the group sought to create a tourist attraction on the isle that would attract wealthy people from all across the Realms. Three months later, things began to go awry for the expedition.

On Marpenoth 11, five velociraptors attacked the expedition's campsite and the team managed to slay them with minimal casualties. The next day Jarkus left with the Chultan Wedonai and the mage Briella Farthing to check on the Sacred Heart of the Beast, an ancient Sunite relic the expedition had brought with them that was supposed to have tamed and made docile all of the animals on the island. By Marpenoth 15 they reached the summit of the volcano where the relic had been set up, encountering the hierophant druid Baerwald, who engaged them in a conversation about how their group was threatening the balance of nature on the island and should depart. The discussion went on to take a violent turn and both Jarkus and Wedonai tried to attack the druid, but were ultimately immobilized and left trapped within a cavern beneath the island that was home to a colony of olive slimes.

Sometime between then and Marpenoth 23, the two men died from the olive slimes and metamorphosed into slime creatures. Their prone bodies were later discovered by a group of adventurers hired by Briella's father Maskin. The two bodies sprung to life as the adventurers got close to them and had to be fought off, finally ending their existence.

## Jaris

Jaris was a wealthy man who came into ownership of one of the buildings within the Crossroad Keep in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Jaris was a pale-skinned human man with bright blonde slicked-back hair. His demeanor, clothes, and appearance all loudly advertised his wealth and complete lack of any hardships in his life.

History
Shortly after the, Jaris inherited a home in the Crossroad Keep on the Sword Coast North. The building was an alchemical laboratory that once belonged to Jaris's uncle. Shortly after the Crossroad Keep welcomed the Mercantile House led by Sa'Sani of Samarach, Jaris arrived at the keep but found the laboratory a mess. His uncle hoarded dangerous and volatile substances and chemicals that could easily damage the property if handled by someone unlearned. He attempted to hire a group of adventurers to handle the situation, but it was unrecorded how much damage, if any, they dealt to the alchemist's home in the process.

## Jacoby

Jacoby was a master blacksmith who ran a smithy in the Crossroad Keep on the Sword Coast North in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Jacoby had the appearance of a typical Tethyrian man – busy skin, brown eyes, and dark hair. He wore his curly hair trimmed short and a well-maintained thin mustache. His face was permanently blushed with the sun from years of hard and determined work. Staring into hot metal left his lips dried and cracked, and hearing diminished. Despite his somewhat rugged appearance, his eyes gleamed with passion every time he examined a masterfully crafted new weapon.

History
Jacoby opened his forge in the fortress of the Crossroad Keep shortly after it was reclaimed by Neverwinter with the aid of the soon-to-be-famous Knight-Captain known as Kalach-Cha. Jacoby continued his work in service of the fortress and its occupants in the years that followed the Shadow War, eventually leading his services to the merchant name Sa'Sani when she was granted residence within Crossroad Keep.

## Jared Rheloph

Jared Rheloph, nicknamed the Pious, was a human cleric of Ilmater and adventurer and a member of the Company of the Sundered Orc in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

History
Five adventurers—Lorar, Pelops, Jared Rheloph, Cassandra Rulmac, and Wynara Rulmac—came together in Daggerford relatively recently as of 1368 DR and soon headed for Waterdeep, not yet even having a name for their company. En route, they encountered a small orc raiding band and destroyed them, so they named themselves for this event.

In Waterdeep, they went to the Yawning Portal inn and began exploring the dungeons of Undermountain as adventurers do. In the, they elected to locate a supposed treasure hoard on the second level, the Arcane Chambers, which the Waterdhavian merchant-adventurer Essimuth Lanys claimed to have sighted. They ventured down the well just after sunset on Eleint 2, and had not yet returned by the end of the month. The Tymoran priest Adama Miiralin stationed at the inn recorded this in his monthly report to High Priestess Seenroas Halvinhar.

In fact, all five of them had been captured by drow slavers in the Arcane Chambers and were soon to be transported to Skullport.

## Jatlo

Jatlo was a redcap servant of the green hag named Auntie Ethel in the city of Baldur's Gate in late 15 century DR.

Description
In his redcap form, Jatlo's appearance was similar to most other members of his fey race. When he took on the guise of a dragonborn, Jatlo appeared as a sinister-looking green-scaled dragon humanoid with no fins nor horns on his snake-like face.

History

In the, Jatlo was ordered by his mistress to infiltrate the Anti-Hag Support Group formed by Auntie Ethel's victim – Mayrina. The redcap penned a letter to the support group asking to join them, creating a story of how his brother was a fellow victim of an evil hag. Jatlo claimed that his bother asked a hag for fame and wealth and was turned into a statue in his hometown's square with a plaque that read "'Toss a Coin for Luck." When Jatlo was allowed to join, he placed a curse on Mayrina using the hag's hex doll – a payback from Ethel. With Mayrina out of the picture, the redcap was trying to find a book that help the recipe for hag's bane – a concoction that could save the life of a child devoured by a hag.

It was unknown if Jatlo was successful or if he was found out and slain by the Absolute Adversaries, who themselves as a bone to pick with Auntie Ethel.

## Janina

Description
Janina was a human woman with fair skin tone and blonde hair.

History
Janina was a mother who loved his son but she was deeply unhappy in life. She felt remorse over her feelings and self-destructive behavior. Janina planned to run away from her family and found a handsome man who offered her a trip away from the city. The woman collected coin to pay for the passage into the life she thought to be worth living. However, that was not to be. Janina died sometime in or shortly before the  as was evident by her body's lack of decay. She was buried in the Lower City Graveyard, not far from its mortuary. Janina grave may had been accidentally desecrated, and her body risen as a zombie by Nina Dortmell, a talented child with a gift, who was trying to resurrect her recently deceased brother.

## Jack wyvern tail

Jack wyvern tail, also known as Jack's wyvern tail and sometimes old wyvern tail, was a Cormyrean dish designed to imitate the taste and texture of the meat from the tail of a wyvern.

Description
The dish was a blend of chopped pork, turkey, and rabbit meat. It had to be prepared carefully to approximate the succulence of true wyvern meat, but was not a perfect substitute. It was eaten either as a hot entree or as cold slices with bread.

The same spices traditionally used when preparing wyvern tail were used in the preparation of jack wyvern tail, sometimes including asparagus broth and almonds.

Availability
With a decline in the population of wyverns in the Stonelands as of the mid–14 century DR, jack wyvern tail grew in popularity as real wyvern tail became harder to acquire in Cormyr.

The Old Oyster restaurant in Monksblade was famous for its "old wyvern tail", which was made with a secret recipe and served in the size and shape of a wyvern's tail with skin made of crispy pork crackling. It was served over a bed of fried potatoes covered in mushroom-cream sauce, and was big enough for four full meals.

Personality
The man was effusive and fond of table-talk.

History
When the elven wizard Ilbryn Starym passed through Ripplestones in early-to-mid-spring of the, he tried to ask Baerdagh and Caladaster Daermree, who were just outside the Fair Maid tavern, about the whereabouts of Elminster Aumar. They did not respond, but a man at the door, introducing himself as Jalobal, blabbed instantly and told him all about the Dead Place where Elminster had gone, east of Ripplestones. Despite Jalobal's warnings, Ilbryn just showed them the wounds on his side, and then went off. Jalobal went back in to tell everyone about it.

## Jarus Brithandir

Jarus Brithandir was an unfortunate Cormyrean Purple Dragon knight adventurer in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Jarus was a large muscular human man with long brown hair, adorned in a Purple Dragon knight's armor and armed with a greatsword.

History

Around the, Jarus Brithandir and his fellow adventurers Helayna, Popkin Shortshanks, Liantor Stoneborn, Kirina Aelorothi, and Tristran of Daggerford found an ancient tomb at the bottom of a crevasse in the Stonelands of Cormyr. They began dungeon-delving the complex and overcame some undead defenders before heading further in through a hidden passageway. They found a long series of caves leading deep underground, overcame more hostile inhabitants, and were eventually ambushed by a team of Clan Daeronnax kobolds that served the great red dragon Daeronnax. The party was split up during the intense fighting, and only Popkin Shortshanks managed to escape the caves entirely. Unable to rescue her friends on her own, she immediately traveled to Eveningstar to find help.

Popkin found a second party of adventurers willing to assist, and sent them on to the tomb to find her friends. The adventurers discovered Jarus Brithandir after a fierce battle in which he slew a band of walking dead and perched himself on a pile of stones to catch his breath. They gave Jarus a trinket enchanted with the word of recall spell that swept him back to the safety of Eveningstar.

## Jar of preserving

A jar of preserving was a type of enchanted jar that allowed for one to preserve any type of material indefinitely.

Description
These jars were round in shape and made of glass, with glass lids that screwed into place. They weighed around  and could hold up to  of liquid or  of material.In 2 edition it was stated that the jar of preserving  could hold up to  of material. In 3 edition, the value and unit of measurement was changed to . Through conversion we can bridge the gap between these two values and units, finding that 1 cubic foot is equivalent to 7 gallons and 2 gallons are equivalent to 0.2673611111 cubic feet.

Powers
A jar of preserving put any type of material or small creature that was placed within it into a state of suspended animation. This meant that any plant matter would never wilt or rot; creatures would never age, hunger, or thirst; and spell components would never lose their potency. Some later versions were found to lack the capacity to keep creature's alive, but kept their corpses from rotting.

Creation
Creating a jar of preserving often required use of the spell gentle repose.

Notable Owners
 These magical jars were a common magical item among necromancers.

History

At some point prior to the, Jaskir was tasked with safely delivering the sage Shandeir to Elturel. They were accompanied on this journey by Falaster Fisk and Sylvira Savikas. They chartered passage with the White Sails Company aboard the Hesperornis. When the ship detoured to Mintarn, Jaskir protested but was overruled by Shandeir. ## Jandove Midler

Jandove Midler was a member of the extended Midler-Chandler family in the mid-1480s DR, who lived together in the Midler farmhouse near Eveningstar.

Description
Jandove was a middle-aged woman with a bob of brown hair, dressed in a long gray dress.

Relationships
Jandove was the child of Wilaver and Caryn Chandler, and sister of Graven. She was the spouse of Ricnan, and mother of their sons Normon and Thoben.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, the drow forces of Vicala Szind attacked the farmhouse and captured the entire family alive so that she could interrogate them about the Thread of the Weave. Unknown to the drow, the houseservant Maisby had escaped back to Eveningstar and sought help, getting first a pair of Purple Dragons and then a team of adventurers to investigate the farmhouse. Unfortunately, Vicala Szind had set all ten Midlers and Chandlers as part of a sacrificial life-draining spell, and the rescuers would have had to stealthily rescue each family member without Vicala noticing, lest she spitefully kill them.

Jandove was kept contained by magical paralysis in the master bedroom with her brother Graven, under the watch of a few disgruntled drow. When saved, she showed the rescuers a passage into the attic space that would allow them to better sneak around the prowling priestess.

## Jarilith

Jariliths were powerful tanar'ri demons considered some of the most dangerous hunters in the Abyss.

Description
Jariliths resembled large male lions with slightly oversized teeth and claws. Their demonic nature was made evident by their blood-red fur.

Abilities
Jariliths were hunters, and their natural gifts broadly reflected that: they were exceptionally good at hiding and moving silently, were comfortable in a variety of terrains ranging from forest to desert and wastes (or the versions thereof found in the Abyss), and had both darkvision and a keen sense of smell. They had at-will magical abilities that were similar to the spells clairaudience/clairvoyance, darkness, detect good, detect thoughts, and doom. In addition, once each day a jarilith could attempt to summon another jarilith to its aid.

Shaped as they were by nightmares, their very presence inspired fear. But their primary weapons were themselves: like great cats they stalked, and pounced, and rended their prey apart with claw and fang.

Ecology
Like all tanar'ri, jariliths were native to the Abyss, where they preferred hunting other demons. They were difficult to control and could sense weakness in others, and to them even other demons seemed weak. They were recorded to live alone, in pairs, or in prides of up to ten adults. They understood the Abyssal language but did not speak; among themselves they communicated via telepathy.

Jariliths were among the creatures used as hunting beasts by more powerful entities visiting the Abyssal layer of Spirac.

History
A number of jariliths served the Lords of Dust as they plotted to free the Spinner of Shadows in Eberron, and also defended the Spinner in her prison when the Church of the Silver Flame attempted to stop her from escaping.

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the mid-1480s DR, jariliths were among the summoned monsters used as gladiatorial beasts in the Underdark Arena of the red dragon Aptoryx.

## Jagen Marsk

Jagen Marsk was a nobleman, wizard, and magic researcher from the nation of Turmish in the Vilhon Reach, who created the rings of resistance sometime before the 14 century DR.

History
Being a mage, Jagen of Turmish was dumbfounded by the limitations on wizards being unable to wear more than two enchanted rings at a time. He owned a variety of wondrous bands and desired to wield them simultaneously. This desire fueled the nobleman's research into combining various enchantments in one ring. His boldest attempt was combining invisibility with teleportation, but the best he could active were the rings of resistance. Jagen settled on creating rings that offered various types of magical protection, universal protection, and a sources of magical light. Jagen made a living by creating and selling such rings and financed his obsession with creating powerful combinations of powers within rings.

## Jansibal Otharr

Jansibal Otharr was a nobleman from Hastarl in Athalantar in the early-to-mid 3 century DR.

Description
He was short and pouty, with red curly hair he kept oiled and a hairless chest. He had been seen dressed in an open-breasted shirt of flame-orange silk, with a fist-sized golden lion medallion and a very prominent gilt ivory codpiece.

Personality
Jansibal was quick to call for blood over even minor annoyances, or even for his own amusement. He was also notably lusty, to the point of risking the displeasure of the Magelords of Athalantar on occasion.

Relationships
Jansibal was the only scion of the noble Otharr house, and he had a rivalry with Thalorn Selemban. He was often accompanied by two bodyguards and a servant that held a lamp over him, and his purse betimes as well.

History
In the, Jansibal Otharr solicited the services of Shandathe Llaerin, a local pleasure-lass, rudely interrupting her flirting with local thief Eladar the Dark, actually the Prince Elminster Aumar. He almost caused a massacre as another local thief, Farl, took exception to him, but they were interrupted by the arrival of fellow dandy, Thalorn Selemban. They bandied some talk, but ultimately Jansibal simply went to a stall with Shandathe. While he was busy there, Farl and Eladar knocked him out and, with Shandathe's complicity, they lured Thalorn Selemban to the same stall and knocked him out too, robbing them both clean.

In the, the dandy was late in responding to the alarm bells of the Magelords of Athalantar in Athalgard. While running to his post, he injured Nanue Glarmeir, drawing the ire of her relative, Darrigo Trumpettower, who drew steel at him. This drew the attention of Thelorn Selemban, who honed in on him despite the danger. After being fought off, he ran away, but didn't get too far before Helm Stoneblade, one of the leaders of the invasion, struck him down, openly surprised at how easy this was.

## Jan Jo's tea kettle

Jan Jo's tea kettle was an enchanted silver kettle that could brew three different types of curative teas depending upon what was boiled inside of it.

Powers
If just plain water was boiled within the kettle, it would come out as a mint-flavored tea that relieved imbibers of all fatigue and tiredness.

Once per day, if actual mint leaves were boiled in the water alongside honey, the kettle would produce one to four cups of tea that had the same effect as a potion of healing. However, this tea had to be consumed very soon after being poured in order for the effect to take place.

Finally, if belladonna was boiled in water within Jan Jo’s tea kettle, the resulting tea had the effect of fully curing an imbiber of lycanthropy. However, for those with a poor constitution, imbibing the tea could cause such a drastic shock to the lycanthrope's physical systems as to result in death. This fatal outcome was of little concern to the tea kettle's creator, as their guarantee was that the kettle would ultimately cure lycanthropy "one way or another".

In the aftermath of the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Jarra was distressed to find that hostile blights were emerging from the nearby Mielikki's Grove. While it was obvious to suspect the local hag Granny Gertrude, Jarra couldn't say for sure that the hag was guilty, and so sent adventurers to investigate the matter and discern what was really going on.

In the end it was revealed that Granny Gertrude was innocent, and the culprit was in fact the Red Wizards of Thay Dhovras, who Granny helped to defeat. Jarra had to admit that sometimes having a hag around was a good thing.

## Jafar

Jafar was a half-orc pirate captain and leader of the Bloody Buccaneers and a member of the Rundeen active on the Sea of Swords and the Shining Sea of west Faerûn in the late 14 century DR.

Possessions
He was captain of the cutter Water Weird.

Activities
They targeted non-Rundeen-affiliated ships transporting trade goods south into the Shining Sea. What cargoes they took were given to the Rundeen in return for rewards and the goods were then sold on by Rundeen businesses. Although they worked hard to make good the "bloody" part of their name, they struggled to kill enough people to get a reputation, as many ships they attacked and raided managed to escape. Nevertheless, they were relatively successful in their piracy and served the Rundeen's ends.

History
Since about 1366 DR, the Bloody Buccaneers had successfully attacked and raided over eight ships. Jafar was formerly first mate on the Water Weird, serving under its captain and co-leader of the pirates.

Then, in the, ships owned by the Bladesmile and Bormul merchant houses of Amn fought back against a raid, even killing the captain of the Water Weird. Jafar succeeded him to the rank of captain and co-leadership of the pirates, and he and Jhamil al Tassand, his fellow captain and brother to the slain captain, swore to have their revenge on Bladesmile and Bormul.

## Jacaman

Jacaman was a small island within the Korinn Archipelago in the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
Jacaman was the furthest east of the notable islands in the Korinn Archipelago, located among a cluster of smaller isles due east of Dennik. It was regarded as Dennik's smaller and uglier cousin.

Trade
Jacaman produced exotic spices for export, and was the premier location among the Korinn islands for the growing of such cash crops. Many of these spices were said to grow only on Jacaman, and were thus highly coveted by merchants. However, trade for these spices was risky owing to the great interest in them shown by pirates.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, control over the island was seized by Roquern, who took over control of cultivation and export of many of the isle's unique spices. He secretly tainted his spices with infernal power that made imbibers more susceptible to coercive magic, and any who consumed large amounts of these spices would become Roquern's thralls. To conceal his operations, he took great pains to convince the outside world that Jacaman was insignificant.

Inhabitants
The isle's Northlander human settlements were known to be weak and unfortunate due to the great deal of pirate activity around the island and its shipping routes. It was not very populous even compared to other islands in the Korinn Archipelago.

By the late 15 century DR, the isle was also home to a large number of evil creatures and slaves in service to Roquern.

Appendix
References

Connections

## Jarlathin Mantlepard

Lord Jarlathin Mantlepard was a nobleman of House Mantlepard in Cormyr in the late 14 century DR, husband to Dethelle Mantlepard, and father to Ileireya Mantlepard and Graer Mantlepard.

Description
In the late 14 century DR, Jarlathin was a fat and big-boned older man.

Possessions
Lord Mantlepard owned a grand manse in Suzail, a modest townhouse in the town of Marsember, and a massive country estate just south of Monksblade.

Relationships
Lord Jarlathin Mantlepard was married to a much younger woman, Dethelle, who was a well-known actress and the mother of Ileireya Mantlepard. Dethelle was Jarlathin's third wife and a successful head of House Mantlepard. Jarlathin's heir was his firstborn son from the first marriage to Yimgraetha Mantlepard, Graer Mantlepard. Darndrella Mantlepard née Horncastle, was Jarlathin's second wife, who left him for another man, and the marriage was dissolved by royal decree.

History
Some twenty years after the death of his first wife, Lord Jarlathin Mantlepard was rumored to have been assassinated in Sembia in the 1370s DR—according to some theories, by his own wife and daughter. The Mantlepard women were said to have been smuggling Lord Mantlepard's body back to the family crypt hidden underneath the family mansion's garden. Ileireya, Jarlathin's daughter, was said to have participated in actual duels over her father's corpse, including a duel against Ezegult Lareer of Selgaunt, the so-called "Lord Skulls." Even more rumors claimed that when Purple Dragons stopped the coach galloping across Cormyr's borders, they found Lord Mantlepard's body bound, buried under some dirt, and stuffed full of stolen rubies. These preposterous rumors quickly spread across the gossip-plastered upper-crust circles of Suzail in Cormyr.

The truth of Jarlathin's death, however, was more mundane: he perished in Yhaunn of heartstop while attending a late-night revelry in one of the city's lavish clubs called The Spouting SeaLyon during the family's month-long visit to Sembia. The details of the revelry were not revealed; however, the nature of the club suggested Lord Mantlepard's last hours of life were filled with expensive wine and the affections of "delicate-cloak ladies." Soon after the Lord's demise, his wife and daughter transported the body to one of Yhaunn's temples, where his demise was confirmed. Then, Jarlathin's body was loaded into the family coach, and the grieving family returned to Cormyr.

The family's attempt to quietly smuggle Lord Jarlathin's body back to Cormyr was the reason for the slew of rumors. This was done by his wife and daughter in an attempt to postpone the news of his death hitting the streets, to be able to move some of the Lord's portable wealth elsewhere before his firstborn son and heir, Lord Graer Mantlepard, could put a claim on it.

The truth of the Purple Dragons' strange discovery was the fact that Lady Dethelle Mantlepard was a skilled thief, who'd robbed numerous jewelers of Yhaunn and stuffed the loot down her dead husband's throat to smuggle them into Cormyr. The dirt that covered Jarlathin's body was simply Dethelle's attempt to stave off decomposition during a long summer ride back home. In Selgaunt, a passing water-carter sprayed the earth-covered cargo with water in jest, uncovering part of Jarlathin's body, which caused some commotion that prompted the local law officials to chase the coach through the city. The coach found shelter in a gated manse belonging to Lord Ezegult, a local crazed necromancer, who insisted the ladies join him for supper. Of course, when the body was discovered by the necromancer's stable servant, an argument broke out as Lord Ezegult wanted to claim the body. Ileireya Mantlepard defended her father's corpse in a sword-duel. Ileireya won, secured the corpse, and ended up having the "Lord Skulls" as her admirer. The necromancer used his undead servants to distract the officers and help the Mantlepard coach to escape Selgaunt.

Another duel arose over Lord Mantlepard, this time when Ileireya fought off a brigand and ended his life in the process. The coach continued on and rushed past a Purple Dragon border post. The Ladies Mantlepard claimed that the body was bound hands and feet by the necromancer before they could stop him. This was suspicious enough to have them detained and interrogated by the post commander Lionar Abdrin Baelbrook. When the body was being inspected by a War Wizard, Lord Jarlathin Mantlepard spilled the gems hidden in his throat. The War Wizard named Jestrel Halroaltaen was tasked with the interrogation, who, as it turned out, was a childhood friend and admirer of Lady Ileireya Mantlepard. Jestrel took the suspected murderers into his custody and disappeared along with them.

Jarlathin Mantlepard's body was left in the Purple Dragons' custody. The gems were collected and disappeared. Soon after, the body disappeared as well. To complicate things further, the Ladies Mantlepard declared Lord Jarlathin Mantlepard to be alive and well before going into hiding, making his heir, Lord Graer's claims on House Mantlepard unlawful. Lord Graer's hirelings, as well as Suzail's Highknights, were searching for Jarlathin and both Ladies Mantlepard.
